# Government and intergovernmental reactions to the Russian invasion of Ukraine

See also: [Non-government reactions to the Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-government_reactions_to_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) and [Reactions to the 2021–2022 Russo-Ukrainian crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reactions_to_the_2021%E2%80%932022_Russo-Ukrainian_crisis)

For broader coverage of this topic, see [Reactions to the 2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reactions_to_the_2022_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine).

The [2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine](79400268.md) received widespread international condemnation, leading to [new sanctions being imposed on Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_sanctions_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War), which triggered [a Russian financial crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_impact_of_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine#Russia).[^cite_note-1] Reactions among governments have most often been negative, with criticism and condemnation, particularly in [Europe](9239.md), the [Americas](29833.md), and [Southeast Asia](28741.md).

International organizations such as the [United Nations](31769.md) (UN) and the [North Atlantic Treaty Organization](21133.md) (NATO) have also voiced significant criticism of the invasion, over it not having justifiable precedent. From the early phases of the invasion, the [United Nations General Assembly](31957.md) voted to condemn Russia for its invasion of Ukraine.

## Ukraine

Ukrainian President [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) called for nations to either establish a [no-fly zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No-fly_zone) over [Ukraine](31750.md) or provide Ukraine with air support.[^cite_note-Basu-2] Zelenskyy also called for "peace", stating that he does not "want Ukraine's history to be [a legend about 300 Spartans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Thermopylae)."[^cite_note-Basu-2] Zelenskyy, in an address to the [British House of Commons](13828.md), also invoked the words of [Winston Churchill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winston_Churchill) by saying "We will fight at sea; we will fight in the air; we will protect our land. We will fight everywhere ... and we will not surrender."[^cite_note-3]

Ukrainian officials have published photos and videos of killed and captured Russian soldiers.[^cite_note-4] Some experts have argued that Article 13 of the [Third Geneva Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_Geneva_Convention) prohibits videos of captured soldiers.[^cite_note-5]

On 2 April, Zelenskyy warned Ukrainian residents that Russian forces retreating from around Kyiv were "mining the whole territory. They are mining homes, mining equipment, even the bodies of people who were killed", and leaving behind "a lot of trip wires, a lot of other dangers."[^cite_note-6]

## Russia

On 26 February 2022, the Russian communications regulator, [Roskomnadzor](15773661.md), ordered independent media outlets to take down reports that described the Russian invasion of Ukraine as an "assault, invasion, or declaration of war", threatening fines and blocks.[^cite_note-7] From 1 March, Russian schools started war-themed social studies classes for teenagers based on the Russian government's position on history; one teaching manual (publicized by independent media outlet [MediaZona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MediaZona)) asserted that "genocide" had been occurring in eastern Ukraine for eight years, and that Russia was responding with a "special peacekeeping operation" in Ukraine, which was "not a war".[^cite_note-8]

Russian President [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) on 4 March declared that Russia had "absolutely no ill intentions with regard to our neighbors".[^cite_note-Bella-9] Putin called for other countries to "think about normalizing relations and cooperating normally", stating there was "no need to escalate the situation, impose restrictions".[^cite_note-Bella-9] On 5 March he criticized Ukraine for resisting the invasion, saying, "they are calling into question the future of Ukrainian statehood."[^cite_note-10] Also that day, the [Russian Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs) urged the countries of the European Union and NATO to "stop pumping weapons" to Ukraine, claiming that terrorists might use the weapons against airplanes.[^cite_note-11]

On 7 March, the Russian government adopted a list of countries and regions "taking unfriendly actions against Russia, Russian companies, and citizens" – [Albania](738.md), [Andorra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andorra), [Australia](4689264.md), [United Kingdom](31717.md),[^cite_note-12] all [European Union](9317.md) states, [Iceland](14531.md), [Canada](5042916.md), [Liechtenstein](17810.md), the [Federated States of Micronesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federated_States_of_Micronesia), [Monaco](19261.md), [New Zealand](4913064.md), [Norway](21241.md), [South Korea](27019.md), [San Marino](27248.md), [North Macedonia](23564616.md), [Singapore](27318.md), [United States](3434750.md), [Taiwan](25734.md), [Ukraine](31750.md), [Montenegro](20760.md), [Switzerland](26748.md), and [Japan](15573.md).[^cite_note-13]

Putin on 8 March declared that Russian "conscript soldiers are not participating in hostilities" in Ukraine "and will not participate in them". On 9 March, the [Russian Ministry of Defense](7875884.md) stated that it had "discovered" Russian conscript soldiers participating in the military operation in Ukraine, and that "almost all" of these conscripts had returned to Russia, but some other conscripts had been "captured" in Ukraine.[^cite_note-14]



Russia and the US control 90% of the world's [nuclear weapons](21785.md). Putin warned that "whoever tries to hinder us" in Ukraine would see consequences "you have never seen in your history".

[^cite_note-15]

On 9 March, Russia bombed a [maternity hospital](18753856.md) in [Mariupol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariupol); [Associated Press](18935732.md) journalists on the scene took photos and videos of multiple bloodstained, pregnant mothers leaving the blown-out maternity ward.[^cite_note-pregnant-16] One pregnant woman and her baby died after the bombing.[^cite_note-pregnant-16] Russian officials provided different, shifting stances on the bombing.[^cite_note-Trevelyan-17] Kremlin spokesman [Dmitry Peskov](9214683.md) initially stated that Russia does "not fire on civilian targets", then later said he lacked "clear information about what happened".[^cite_note-Trevelyan-17] Foreign Minister [Sergey Lavrov](541346.md) criticized "pathetic shouting about so-called atrocities", stating that the hospital had no patients and doctors. According to him, it was controlled by Ukrainian extremists.[^cite_note-Trevelyan-17] Russia's Defense Ministry denied bombing the hospital, and accused Ukraine of staging the bombing.[^cite_note-Trevelyan-17] Russian officials called images of the attack "fake news" and labelled a pregnant woman pictured fleeing the bombed hospital as an actor.[^cite_note-pregnant-16]

On 16 March, a Russian airstrike [hit a theatre in Mariupol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariupol_theatre_airstrike) containing hundreds of civilians; satellite pictures three days prior showed large words "DETI" ("children" in Russian) displayed as a signal to Russian forces about those inside the theatre.[^cite_note-Rosa-18] Russia's military denied bombing the theatre.[^cite_note-Rosa-18] Russia's foreign ministry said that it was a "lie" that Russia bombed the theatre, insisting that "Russia's armed forces don't bomb towns and cities".[^cite_note-19] During the invasion, Russia used airstrikes against Ukrainian cities including Mariupol, Kyiv and Kharkiv.[^cite_note-20]

On 16 March, Putin gave a speech calling Russian opponents of the war "scum and traitors,"[^cite_note-21] saying that "natural and necessary self-cleansing of society will only strengthen our country."[^cite_note-22]

On 25 March, the leader of the Russian military's [General Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Staff_of_the_Armed_Forces_of_the_Russian_Federation) Main Operational Directorate, [Sergei Rudskoi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergei_Rudskoi), stated that the first stage of the operation had "generally been accomplished", and the "combat potential of the Armed Forces of Ukraine (was) considerably reduced", allowing Russia "to focus our core efforts on achieving the main goal, the liberation of [Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donbas)" in eastern Ukraine.[^cite_note-23] A day later, Russian forces bombed [Lviv](49968.md), a city in western Ukraine.[^cite_note-24]



&#x20; Countries that have banned [Russian aircraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_biggest_airlines_of_Russia) from their airspace. Russia's retaliatory ban on aircraft from 36 countries changed global airline routes.

On 1 April, Russia accused Ukraine of conducting an airstrike on Russian territory; this was the first such accusation since the start of the Russian invasion. Russia said that an airstrike on a [fuel depot](3385681.md) in [Belgorod](478861.md) had upset the peace negotiations with Ukraine.[^cite_note-25]

After Russian forces occupied [Bucha, Kyiv Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bucha,_Kyiv_Oblast) for five weeks and then withdrew, they were [accused by Bucha officials of extrajudicial killings of Bucha residents](70455343.md). Russia's Ministry of Defense responded on 3 April that "not a single local resident suffered from any violent actions" during the Russian occupation of Bucha.[^cite_note-26] This claim was contradicted by many eyewitness accounts by Bucha residents.[^cite_note-27]

The Russian Ministry of Defence suggested that corpses of dead civilians had been placed on the streets of Bucha after "all Russian units withdrew completely" from the city on 30 March, but satellite photos were taken by [Maxar Technologies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maxar_Technologies) showed that at least 11 body-sized objects on Bucha's Yablonska Street appeared between 9 and 11 March, remaining there for more than three weeks, in the same positions as 11 civilian corpses in a 1 April video.[^cite_note-28] A high-ranking Russian official, Mikhail Ulyanov, claimed that Bucha's mayor Anatoliy Fedoruk did not discuss "dead bodies in the streets" in a 31 March video, but Fedoruk on 7 March had already done so, and on 28 March, accused Russian forces of killings and rapes in Bucha.[^cite_note-29]

[RIA Novosti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RIA_Novosti), a media outlet controlled and owned by the Russian government, published an article by Timofey Sergeytsev that declared that "Nazis who took weapons must be killed in numbers as much as possible", while "most of the \[Ukrainian] people are guilty, they are passive Nazis, Nazi enablers ... and must be punished". The article also stated that Ukraine "may develop only in dependency to Russia", and that "history has proven Ukraine may not exist as a national state".[^cite_note-30][^cite_note-31][^cite_note-32] On 5 April 2022, Russia's opposition politician [Alexei Navalny](29174356.md) said the "monstrosity of lies" in the [Russian state media](15861222.md) "is unimaginable. And, unfortunately, so is its persuasiveness for those who have no access to alternative information."[^cite_note-33] He tweeted that "warmongers" among Russian state media personalities "should be treated as war criminals. From the editors-in-chief to the talk show hosts to the news editors, \[they] should be sanctioned now and tried someday."[^cite_note-34] In July 2022, [Alexei Gorinov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexei_Gorinov), a member of the [Krasnoselsky district council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krasnoselsky_District,_Moscow) in Moscow, was sentenced to seven years in prison after making anti-war comments at a council meeting in March,[^cite_note-35] including stating that "our country has aggressively attacked a neighbouring country" and "kids in Ukraine are dying each day".[^cite_note-36] Lawyer [Pavel Chikov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pavel_Chikov) said that this was the first jail term under the new [Russian 2022 war censorship laws](70646883.md).[^cite_note-37]

## Other countries



South African President [Cyril Ramaphosa](1089194.md) promoted the [African peace plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_negotiations_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine)



In April 2023, Brazilian President [Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva](99328.md) condemned Russia's violation of Ukraine's territorial integrity and said Russia should withdraw from Ukrainian territory it has [occupied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian-occupied_territories_of_Ukraine) since February 2022.

[^cite_note-38]

| State | Notes |
| --- | --- |
| [Albania](738.md) | President of Albania [Ilir Meta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilir_Meta) convened the National Security Council and issued a statement to "strongly condemn Russia's military attack on Ukraine" as an "unprovoked and unjustifiable escalation" while expressing sorrow for lives lost and "full solidarity with the people of Ukraine, and its democratic institutions".[^cite_note-40] Similar statements were made earlier by Prime Minister [Edi Rama](277805.md) on Twitter,[^cite_note-prishtinainsight.com-41] Minister for Europe and Foreign Affairs [Olta Xhaçka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olta_Xha%C3%A7ka),[^cite_note-42] and Ambassador to the UN [Ferit Hoxha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferit_Hoxha) at the Security Council meeting,[^cite_note-43] joined the United States in calling for a UN vote on a resolution condemning Russian actions, with the intention of forcing Russia to veto.[^cite_note-44] After a NATO summit, Rama said that Albania was ready to welcome a few thousand Ukrainian refugees fleeing the war.[^cite_note-45] |
| [Andorra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andorra) | Prime Minister [Xavier Espot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xavier_Espot) appealed for peace, saying that "war should be no recourse".[^cite_note-46] On 2 March, Andorra joined economic sanctions against Russia. Andorra had never previously applied sanctions towards another country.[^cite_note-47] |
| [Antigua and Barbuda](951.md) | Minister of Foreign Affairs [Paul Chet Greene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Chet_Greene) condemned the Russian invasion and urged diplomatic talks.[^cite_note-48] |
| [Argentina](18951905.md) | On 24 February, the [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_and_Worship) of [Argentina](18951905.md) called on Russia to respect the charter of the [United Nations](31769.md) and international law.[^cite_note-49] President [Alberto Fernández](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alberto_Fern%C3%A1ndez) asked "the Russian Federation to put an end to the military action, respect Ukraine's sovereignty and return to dialogue."[^cite_note-50] On 24 February, Argentina, along with [Brazil](3383.md) and three other Latin American states, abstained from a vote at the [Organization of American States](22788.md) condemning the Russian invasion of Ukraine, saying the organization was not a "pertinent forum."[^cite_note-51] On 2 March, Argentina voted in favor of the [United Nations General Assembly Resolution ES-11/1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_ES-11/1) condemning Russia's invasion of Ukraine and demanding a full withdrawal of Russian forces.[^cite_note-52] Before the UN, Foreign Minister [Santiago Cafiero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santiago_Cafiero) said that the invasion of Ukraine was "illegitimate" and condemned "military operations on Ukrainian soil," arguing further that the world "does not tolerate more deaths or wars."[^cite_note-53] Argentina has also supported Ukraine at the UN Human Rights Council.[^cite_note-UN_Human_Rights_Council-54] On 1 July 2022, Fernández had a phone conversation with President Zelenskyy, where Fernández condemned the invasion again and promised more aid to Ukraine.[^cite_note-55] After the change of government in December 2023, newly inaugurated president [Javier Milei](56599840.md) took a much harsher stance towards Russia.[^cite_note-56] Milei met with Zelenskyy, and authorized the transfer of two military helicopters to Ukraine, promising more military aid.[^cite_note-57][^cite_note-58] |
| [Australia](4689264.md) | Prime Minister [Scott Morrison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scott_Morrison) denounced the invasion and imposed export controls and travel bans on Russia, as a price for the "unprovoked, unlawful, unwarranted, unjustified attacks and threats and intimidation" and reaffirmed Australia's "unwavering commitment to Ukraine's sovereignty and territorial integrity". However, Morrison suggested that he did not expect Putin to be deterred. In response to Russia's claim of peacekeeping, Australia replied "They're not peacekeepers. They're invaders".[^cite_note-59] On 25 February, Morrison condemned China's easing of import restrictions on Russian wheat as "inexplicable" and "completely unacceptable", and said it gave a "lifeline to Russia ... while they're invading another country".[^cite_note-60] Minister for Communications [Paul Fletcher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Fletcher_\(politician\)) called for a suspension on broadcasts of two Russian state-controlled TV networks, RT and [NTV Moscow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NTV_Moscow), on 25 February; two Australian TV services suspended broadcasts of RT, one of which also suspended broadcasts of NTV Moscow.[^cite_note-61][^cite_note-62] |
| [Austria](26964606.md) | Chancellor of Austria [Karl Nehammer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karl_Nehammer) said "There is war in Europe again", condemned Russia's attack and declared Austria's solidarity with Ukraine.[^cite_note-63] |
| [The Bahamas](3451.md) | The Bahamas denounced the invasion and Foreign Minister [Fred Mitchell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fred_Mitchell_\(Bahamian_politician\)) stated "The invasion of Ukraine by the Russian Federation and led by its President Vladimir Putin is wrong, unlawful and should end and be reversed."[^cite_note-64] |
| [Barbados](3455.md) | Prime Minister [Mia Mottley](15337866.md) urged Russia to withdraw and described the invasion as a violation of Ukrainian territorial integrity.[^cite_note-65] |
| [Belgium](3343.md) | Prime Minister [Alexander De Croo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_De_Croo) said the EU needed "Russian sanctions that bite", especially against the "ruling elite".[^cite_note-66] In early April, three Ukrainian families were to be placed in two of the [Belgian royal family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgian_royal_family)'s properties, managed by the [Royal Trust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Trust_\(Belgium\)), while a third property would be used for "collective accommodation of Ukrainian refugees".[^cite_note-67] |
| [Belize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belize) | The [Government of Belize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Belize) condemned the 'illegal Russian invasion' and expressed its solidarity with Ukraine.[^cite_note-68] [Prince William](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_William), the grandson of the [Queen of Belize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_of_Belize), quoted her 1994 speech to the Belizean National Assembly, and said that people in Belize stood in solidarity with those struggling in Ukraine.[^cite_note-69] |
| [Bhutan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhutan) | Bhutan voted for [United Nations General Assembly Resolution ES-11/1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_ES-11/1), condemning the invasion.[^cite_note-The_Diplomat-70] |
| [Botswana](3464.md) | Botswana was one of 87 signatories to the UN letter condemning Russia's invasion of Ukraine.[^cite_note-Axios-71] A year after the invasion of Ukraine, *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)* downgraded Botswana from "West leaning" to "Russia leaning".[^cite_note-Who_are_Russia's_supporters-72] |
| [Brunei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brunei) | Brunei condemned the invasion, calling for the situation to be resolved without force.[^cite_note-73] |
| [Bulgaria](3415.md) | Bulgaria condemned the invasion and Prime Minister [Kiril Petkov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiril_Petkov) said: "We see that this aggression was not provoked by the Ukrainian side and such actions in Europe are inadmissible".[^cite_note-74] Bulgarian Minister of Defence [Stefan Yanev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefan_Yanev) was sacked for insisting it was wrong to describe it as a war, saying it was an "operation." He also said there was no need for Bulgaria—a member of both the EU and NATO—to adopt a "pro-Russian, pro-American or pro-European position."[^cite_note-75] |
| [Cambodia](334751.md) | Prime Minister [Hun Sen](393779.md) said that only through peaceful negotiation, and not war, could any disagreements be resolved.[^cite_note-76] Hun Sen condemned the actions of Russia, saying "I still stand in solidarity with Ukrainian people against the invasion."[^cite_note-77] It has been alleged that Cambodia's foreign ministry wanted to remain strictly neutral and abstain at UN votes, similar to Vietnam and Laos, in the days after Russia invaded Ukraine on 24 February last year but Hun Sen intervened and ordered his diplomat at the UN to co-sponsor a resolution condemning Moscow.[^cite_note-78] |
| [Canada](5042916.md) | Prime Minister [Justin Trudeau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Trudeau) condemned "in the strongest possible terms Russia's egregious attack on Ukraine" and stated that "these unprovoked actions are a clear further violation of Ukraine's sovereignty and territorial integrity. They are also in violation of Russia's obligations under international law and the Charter of the United Nations."[^cite_note-79] |
| [Cape Verde](18962637.md) | The Prime Minister of Cape Verde [José Ulisses Correia e Silva](50229553.md) condemned the invasion and called for a search for solutions through diplomatic channels and dialogue.[^cite_note-80][^cite_note-81] |
| [Chile](5489.md) | President [Sebastián Piñera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebasti%C3%A1n_Pi%C3%B1era) said that "Russia's act of aggression and violation of Ukraine's sovereignty" violated international law,[^cite_note-82] while [President](11885300.md) [-elect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/-elect) [Gabriel Boric](47329497.md) "condemn\[ed] the invasion of Ukraine, the violation of its sovereignty and the illegitimate use of force".[^cite_note-83] |
| [Colombia](5222.md) | President [Iván Duque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iv%C3%A1n_Duque) stated that Colombia "categorically rejects the attacks against Ukraine by Russia" and considers the invasion a violation of international law and the United Nations charter.[^cite_note-84] A year after the invasion of Ukraine, *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)* downgraded Colombia from "Condemns Russia" to "neutral".[^cite_note-Who_are_Russia's_supporters-72] |
| [Costa Rica](5551.md) | President [Carlos Alvarado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Alvarado) released a statement on Twitter rejecting and condemning the "use of force and the violation of the sovereignty and territorial integrity of Ukraine", saying that peace is the "only way".[^cite_note-85] |
| [Croatia](5573.md) | Prime Minister [Andrej Plenković](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrej_Plenkovi%C4%87) released a statement on Twitter saying: "We strongly condemn Russia's aggression and invasion on Ukraine. This unprovoked attack is a gross violation of Ukraine's sovereignty and international law".[^cite_note-86] Plenković also met with the Ukrainian Ambassador to Croatia, noting that Croatia would support the EU sanctions package, and stood ready to provide humanitarian and technical assistance to Ukraine.[^cite_note-87] |
| [Cyprus](5593.md) | President [Nicos Anastasiades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicos_Anastasiades) tweeted his condemnation "in the strongest possible terms" adding "I must say that we witness with great disappointment what is happening in violation of international law and we cannot but condemn as a country similar actions that violate the sovereignty and territorial integrity of an independent country". Meanwhile, Foreign Minister [Ioannis Kasoulidis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ioannis_Kasoulidis) called for a ceasefire and said that "these are military operations within Europe, something we have avoided since the end of WWII".[^cite_note-88] |
| [Czech Republic](5321.md) | President [Miloš Zeman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milo%C5%A1_Zeman) on 24 February called the invasion "an unprovoked act of aggression" and said that "Russia has committed a crime against peace", calling for harsh sanctions. He said he had been "wrong" to insist until a few days earlier that Russia would not invade Ukraine.[^cite_note-89] Prime Minister [Petr Fiala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petr_Fiala) said his government had withdrawn its agreement to Russian consulates in [Karlovy Vary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karlovy_Vary) and [Brno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brno), suspended the operation of the consulates of the Czech Republic in [St. Petersburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Petersburg) and [Yekaterinburg](66524.md), and stopped issuing visas for Russian citizens. He also said that the republic would insist on the adoption of the strictest anti-Russian sanctions.[^cite_note-90] |
| [Denmark](76972.md) | Prime Minister [Mette Frederiksen](26040044.md) said that it was a "dark day for peace around the world", while stating that her government was ready to accept Ukrainian refugees.[^cite_note-91]  [Faroe Islands](17238607.md) – Prime Minister, [Bárður á Steig Nielsen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%C3%A1r%C3%B0ur_%C3%A1_Steig_Nielsen) said: "It's a sad day and our thoughts are with the Ukrainian people. This is not only an attack on Ukraine but also on European peace. The Faroe Islands strongly condemn the Russian invasion of Ukraine. The attack is a clear violation of international law, which is meant to maintain justice, security and stability."[^cite_note-92]  [Greenland](12118.md) – Greenland's Prime Minister, [Múte B. Egede](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%BAte_B._Egede), condemned the invasion, called it meaningless, and announced that Greenland would join international sanctions against Russia.[^cite_note-93] |
| [Dominica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominica) | Dominica condemned the invasion and called for an end to the 'aggression'.[^cite_note-94] |
| [Dominican Republic](8060.md) | Dominican President [Luis Abinader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Abinader) released a statement urging Russia to withdraw from Ukraine and furthermore stated that Russia was violating the political, cultural and territorial identity of Ukrainians.[^cite_note-95] |
| [Ecuador](9334.md) | President [Guillermo Lasso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guillermo_Lasso) said that Ecuador would support the position of the United Nations and the Organization of American States in condemning the invasion. "The aggression is a violent intrusion and a violation of our principles for worldwide peace," he said. He added, however, that Ecuador has no plans to suspend diplomatic relations with Russia.[^cite_note-96] |
| [Estonia](28222445.md) | Prime Minister [Kaja Kallas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaja_Kallas) called Russia a "threat to the whole of Europe".[^cite_note-97] [Riigikogu](794927.md), the parliament of Estonia, also passed two statements on the mobilization of Russia's pre-invasion forces and the start of the attack against Ukraine in 2022, in which it expressed support for the territorial integrity of Ukraine and condemned the war started by the Russian Federation.[^cite_note-98][^cite_note-99] |
| [Federated States of Micronesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federated_States_of_Micronesia) | The Federated States of Micronesia [severed diplomatic relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federated_States_of_Micronesia%E2%80%93Russia_relations) with Russia on 25 February 2022. "The FSM affirms its stalwart support of the North Atlantic Treaty Organization and the United Nations, who correctly condemn the Russian invasion of Ukraine. The FSM supports the removal of the Russian Federation in its capacity as president of the United Nations Security Council; Russia remaining there is a façade, as they need to be upholding the international rules-based order instead of undermining it."[^cite_note-100][^cite_note-101] |
| [Fiji](10707.md) | Acting Prime Minister [Aiyaz Sayed-Khaiyum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aiyaz_Sayed-Khaiyum) condemned Russia's actions in Ukraine, calling for an end to all hostilities and violation of international law and urging Russia to "return to the diplomatic table".[^cite_note-102] |
| [Finland](10577.md) | President [Sauli Niinistö](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sauli_Niinist%C3%B6) condemned Russia's attack and President Vladimir Putin, saying "the mask has now come off and only the cold face of war is visible".[^cite_note-103] Prime Minister [Sanna Marin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanna_Marin) said Russia's invasion of Ukraine would change [the debate around NATO membership within her country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finland%E2%80%93NATO_relations), and wrote on Twitter that "The attack is a grave breach of international law and threatens the life of numerous civilians. Finland expresses its solid support to Ukraine and Ukrainians and we are looking for ways to increase this support".[^cite_note-104] On 25 February 2022, a Russian foreign ministry spokesperson threatened "military and political consequences" if Finland attempted to join NATO.[^cite_note-105] Niinistö and Marin issued a joint declaration on 12 May declaring that Finland should join NATO.[^cite_note-106] |
| [France](5843419.md) | President of France Emmanuel Macron said he had spoken with Putin "to stop the fighting and talk to the Ukrainian president"[^cite_note-107] and demanded "an immediate halt to Russian military operations in Ukraine".[^cite_note-108][^cite_note-109] |
| [Gabon](12027.md) | Gabon participated in the Albanian-American-led Joint Statement following a vote on a UN Security Council resolution on Russia's aggression toward Ukraine.[^cite_note-Mureithi-2022-110] A year after the invasion of Ukraine, *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)* downgraded Gabon from "West leaning" to "neutral".[^cite_note-Who_are_Russia's_supporters-72] |
| [The Gambia](65656.md) | The Gambia cosponsored the Albanian-American-led statement at the UN Security Council condemning Russia.[^cite_note-Greenfield-2022-111] |
| [Georgia](48768.md) | President [Salome Zourabichvili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salome_Zourabichvili) stated that "We are participating in all kinds of international financial sanctions and that's quite something for the Georgian financial sector. At the same time, we are participating in all the international resolutions that have been taken to support Ukraine. We share \[with Ukraine] a common two-century history of Russian aggression and we know what that means"[^cite_note-112] Zourabichvili said that her nation was "shocked" by Russian aggression in Ukraine. She affirmed her "solidarity with the Ukrainian people" and called for a halt in military operations.[^cite_note-113] The Georgian government contributed 1 million GEL from its reserve budget to help Ukrainians harmed by Russia's military assault. Prime Minister Irakli Garibashvili made the decision and signed the decree authorizing the aid. The funding will be used to purchase medical supplies for Georgia's Ministry of Internally Displaced Persons, Labor, Health, and Social Affairs.[^cite_note-agenda.ge-114] On 24 February, the Chairman of the Parliament of Georgia [Shalva Papuashvili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shalva_Papuashvili) expressed his solidarity with Ukraine and called on the international community to take steps to "stop Russia escalating into a full-scale conflict and ensure the protection of international norms."[^cite_note-115] On 27 February 100 tons of humanitarian aid were delivered to Ukraine via Poland, including first-aid supplies, over 30 types of pharmaceuticals, oxygen concentrators, and other humanitarian supplies. In addition, Georgia provided blood components, transfusion materials, and diagnostics.[^cite_note-agenda.ge-114] On 4 March, the Georgian Health Ministry declared that all Ukrainian citizens in Georgia would receive free emergency medical services if needed.[^cite_note-agenda.ge-114] Prime Minister [Irakli Garibashvili](37868165.md) said that Georgia's government could not impose separate sanctions against Russia.[^cite_note-116] Vice Prime Minister/Minister of Foreign Affairs of Georgia, [David Zalkaliani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Zalkaliani) stated that "Military aggression launched by Russia against Ukraine is totally intolerable". He called on international partners to give a strict response to the violation of international law by Russia and ensure the de-escalation of the situation.[^cite_note-117] The [Ministry of Foreign Affairs of Georgia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_of_Georgia) expressed extreme concern about developments in Ukraine. According to its statement, Russia's military actions undermined Ukraine's territorial integrity and sovereignty as well as the UN Charter and fundamental principles of international law.[^cite_note-118] Economy Minister Levan Davitashvili said that Georgia was part of every international sanction against Russia[^cite_note-119] A Parliamentary delegation of Georgia visited two Ukrainian cities where Papuashvili strongly opposed Russia's invasion of Ukraine. The opposition United National Movement (UNM), Georgia's main opposition party, paid a surprise visit to Kyiv on the same day, sending a separate delegation that included party chairman Nika Melia and former Georgian President [Giorgi Margvelashvili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giorgi_Margvelashvili).[^cite_note-120] |
| [Germany](11867.md) | [Annalena Baerbock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annalena_Baerbock), minister of foreign affairs, said that the world woke up in a different world. She announced massive sanctions against Russia. Chancellor [Olaf Scholz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olaf_Scholz) called the invasion a "serious mistake" of Putin's.[^cite_note-121] Germany initially ruled out sending weapons to Ukraine and prevented Estonia from sending German-made howitzers to Ukraine.[^cite_note-Telegraph_20220226-122] Germany said it was sending 5,000 helmets and a field hospital to Ukraine,[^cite_note-123] to which Kyiv mayor Vitali Klitschko responded, "What will they send next? Pillows?"[^cite_note-Telegraph_20220226-122] On 26 February, in what was seen by observers as a reaction to mounting pressure from allies in NATO and EU, Germany reversed itself and allowed Estonia to send nine German-made howitzers and the Netherlands to send 400 rocket-propelled grenade launchers, and additionally agreed to send 1,000 anti-tank weapons and 500 Stinger anti-aircraft defence systems to Ukraine.[^cite_note-124] At an emergency parliamentary session on 27 February, Scholz spoke of a "new era" that had begun with the Russian invasion. Germany would from then on invest more than the NATO target of two percent of the gross domestic product in defence. 100 billion euros would be made available for investments in the army in 2022.[^cite_note-125] According to *[Bild](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bild)*, German President [Frank-Walter Steinmeier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank-Walter_Steinmeier) planned to travel to Kyiv in April 2022 as a signal of solidarity but was snubbed by Ukrainian officials.[^cite_note-126] |
| [Ghana](12067.md) | Foreign Minister [Shirley Ayorkor Botchway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shirley_Ayorkor_Botchway) condemned the invasion.[^cite_note-127] |
| [Greece](12108.md) | President [Katerina Sakellaropoulou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katerina_Sakellaropoulou) said that "we strongly condemn the Russian attack on an independent country" as "a clear violation of international law and our values".[^cite_note-128] On 8 March 2022, [International Women's Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Women's_Day), Sakellaropoulou appeared at the [Hellenic Parliament](2449826.md) with a dress in the colors of Ukraine's flag, in tribute to the Ukrainian women affected by the war.[^cite_note-129] Prime Minister [Kyriakos Mitsotakis](2780816.md) condemned the "revisionist" actions of Russia against Ukraine.[^cite_note-130][^cite_note-131][^cite_note-132] |
| [Grenada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grenada) | Grenada condemned the invasion.[^cite_note-133] |
| [Guatemala](17238567.md) | President [Alejandro Giammattei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alejandro_Giammattei) released a statement on Twitter, condemning the Russian invasion.[^cite_note-134] |
| [Guyana](17238691.md) | Guyana condemned the invasion and urged Russia to respect Ukrainian sovereignty.[^cite_note-135][^cite_note-136] |
| [Haiti](13373.md) | Haiti expressed concerns at the situation in Ukraine and urged the two countries to find a diplomatic solution. Haiti cosponsored a UN Security Council resolution condemning Russia.[^cite_note-137][^cite_note-Greenfield-2022-111] |
| [Iceland](14531.md) | Prime Minister [Katrín Jakobsdóttir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katr%C3%ADn_Jakobsd%C3%B3ttir) condemned Russia's invasion of Ukraine as "an unacceptable breach of international law."[^cite_note-138] |
| [Indonesia](14579.md) | [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Indonesia\)) spokesman Teuku Faizasyah expressed concern about the conflict. Indonesia urged Russia to respect the sovereignty and territorial integrity of Ukraine.[^cite_note-139] A ministry press release said that the attack on Ukraine was "unacceptable".[^cite_note-140] [Indonesian President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_President) [Joko Widodo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joko_Widodo) also tweeted "Stop war. War brings suffering to mankind and endangers the world."[^cite_note-141][^cite_note-142][^cite_note-143] The [People's Representative Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Representative_Council) also issued a condemnation statement.[^cite_note-144] |
| [Italy](14532.md) | Prime Minister [Mario Draghi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mario_Draghi) promised "whatever it takes to restore Ukrainian sovereignty" and said it was "impossible to have meaningful dialogue with Moscow", demanding Russia unconditionally pull its forces back to the internationally established borders.[^cite_note-145] |
| [Republic of Ireland](14560.md) | President [Michael D. Higgins](559753.md) called the Russian invasion "unacceptable and immoral" and stated: "This violence must stop. Troops must be withdrawn by Russia. The rise of militarism must end. Full humanitarian access must be given to all civilians in need. Every glimmer of hope through diplomacy must be seized."[^cite_note-146] Taoiseach [Micheál Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miche%C3%A1l_Martin) condemned the "outrageous" actions of Russia in Ukraine and promised "severe sanctions from EU", while saying: "Our thoughts must be with the innocent people of Ukraine at this, their most difficult hour."[^cite_note-147] Tánaiste [Leo Varadkar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leo_Varadkar) stated that whilst Ireland is [militarily neutral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_neutrality), "in this conflict, Ireland is not neutral at all", acknowledging the country's "unwavering and unconditional" support for Ukraine.[^cite_note-148] He compared the invasion of Ukraine to the [invasion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_occupation_of_Czechoslovakia) of [Czechoslovakia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czechoslovakia) in 1939, calling Putin "the Hitler of the 21st century".[^cite_note-149] |
| [Jamaica](15660.md) | Prime Minister [Andrew Holness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Holness) condemned Russia, stating "Jamaica is consistent in its support for the universal respect and adherence to the principles of international law, the respect for the territorial integrity and sovereignty of all nations. We, therefore, cannot support, and, in fact, we condemn the invasion of Ukraine".[^cite_note-150] |
| [Japan](15573.md) | Prime Minister [Fumio Kishida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fumio_Kishida) condemned the invasion and announced that it would cooperate with the United States on further sanctions against Russia.[^cite_note-151] On 25 February, Japan implemented new sanctions, including an export ban on [semiconductors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semiconductor) and other high-tech products, and froze the assets of three Russian banks, but later stated that it would not confiscate the frozen Russian foreign reserves parked with the Bank of Japan.[^cite_note-152] It also considered extending sanctions to Belarus, because of its support for the invasion. On 26 February, Japan agreed to increase deterrence efforts with the U.S., with Japanese Foreign Minister [Yoshimasa Hayashi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoshimasa_Hayashi) stating that the "impact of this will not stop in Europe." [Japan–Russia relations](2951699.md) had previously been characterized by Japanese attempts to avoid antagonizing Russia, particularly in that the [Kuril Islands dispute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuril_Islands_dispute) was Russia's only territorial dispute in Asia. However, Japan was increasingly concerned about the geopolitical implications of the annexation on China and Taiwan.[^cite_note-153][^cite_note-154] |
| [Kenya](188171.md) | Kenya participated in the Albanian-American-led Joint Statement following a vote on a UN Security Council resolution on Russia's aggression toward Ukraine.[^cite_note-Mureithi-2022-110] |
| [Kiribati](16674.md) | Kiribati cosponsored a UN Security Council resolution condemning Russia.[^cite_note-Greenfield-2022-111] |
| [Kuwait](7515890.md) | Kuwait participated in the US-led joint statement following a vote on a UN Security Council resolution on Russia's aggression.[^cite_note-usun.usmission.gov-155] |
| [Latvia](17514.md) | [President of Latvia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Latvia) [Egils Levits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egils_Levits) strongly condemned the Russian invasion of Ukraine, calling for "all possible support, including weapons" to Ukraine and "the harshest possible sanctions" against Russia.[^cite_note-156] In a later interview he called the invasion the "beginning of the end for Putin".[^cite_note-157] On 26 February, the [Ministry of Foreign Affairs of Latvia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Latvia\)) suspended issuing visas to Russian nationals with the exception of [humanitarian visas](50128538.md).[^cite_note-158] Two days later, [Saeima](499765.md) approved legal amendments allowing Latvian nationals to voluntarily fight on the Ukrainian side against the Russian invasion.[^cite_note-159] On 4 March, [Riga City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riga_City_Council) renamed a section of the street in front of the Russian Embassy to Ukrainian Independence Street.[^cite_note-160] [Prime Minister of Latvia](481129.md) [Krišjānis Kariņš](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kri%C5%A1j%C4%81nis_Kari%C5%86%C5%A1) expressed that the [Latvian economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Latvia) and exports should continue to transition away from Russia, and the dependence on Russian energy imports should be reduced as soon as possible.[^cite_note-161] |
| [Lebanon](17771.md) | The [Lebanese Foreign Ministry](30975282.md) on 24 February condemned Russia's invasion, and called on Moscow to "immediately halt military operations."[^cite_note-162] Russia's embassy in Lebanon expressed surprise at this condemnation, releasing a statement that "The statement... surprised us \[the Russian embassy] by violating the policy of dissociation and by taking one side against another in these events, noting that Russia spared no effort in contributing to the advancement and stability of the Lebanese Republic".[^cite_note-163] A year after the invasion of Ukraine, *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)* downgraded Lebanon from "West leaning" to "neutral".[^cite_note-Who_are_Russia's_supporters-72] |
| [Lesotho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lesotho) | Lesotho participated in the Albanian-American-led Joint Statement following a vote on a UN Security Council resolution on Russia's aggression toward Ukraine.[^cite_note-usun.usmission.gov-155] |
| [Liberia](17791.md) | The Liberian government said that the unprovoked attack was unacceptable, and "\[urged] Russia to ease hostilities".[^cite_note-164] |
| [Libya](17633.md) | Foreign Minister [Najla Mangoush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Najla_Mangoush) condemned Russia's military intervention as a violation of international law, and called on Moscow "to calm and retreat."[^cite_note-165] A year after the invasion of Ukraine, *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)* downgraded Libya from "West leaning" to "neutral".[^cite_note-Who_are_Russia's_supporters-72] |
| [Liechtenstein](17810.md) | Liechtenstein condemned the invasion in a statement published on the government's official website.[^cite_note-166] The government of Liechtenstein promised [CHF](361362.md) 500,000 from its existing International Humanitarian Cooperation and Development Budget to humanitarian projects for those affected by the war.[^cite_note-167] |
| [Lithuania](17675.md) | Lithuania strongly condemned the invasion and called for military, economic and humanitarian aid for Ukraine.[^cite_note-168] President of Lithuania [Gitanas Nauseda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gitanas_Nauseda) declared a state of emergency, ordering the NATO country's armed forces to deploy along its borders in response to "possible disturbances and provocations due to large military forces massed in Russia and Belarus".[^cite_note-169] |
| [Luxembourg](17515.md) | Prime Minister [Xavier Bettel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xavier_Bettel) condemned the invasion on Twitter.[^cite_note-170] Foreign minister [Jean Asselborn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean_Asselborn), in an interview with local radio, said the "physical elimination" of Putin was potentially the only way out of the war. He later described his remarks as a "slip of the tongue" and an emotional reaction to having just heard of Russia's indiscriminate attacks on Kharkiv.[^cite_note-171] |
| [Malawi](19088.md) | President [Lazarus Chakwera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lazarus_Chakwera) urged Russia to withdraw.[^cite_note-172] |
| [Malaysia](3607937.md) | Prime Minister [Ismail Sabri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ismail_Sabri) expressed concern over the "escalation of conflict in Ukraine". He called for dialogue and "peace and security".[^cite_note-173] Independent news portal [Malaysiakini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malaysiakini) noted that the statement lacked forceful language and did not mention Russia or call the conflict as an "invasion", prompting a response accusing the news portal of diverting from "the gist of the press statement".[^cite_note-174] On 27 February, the [Malaysian Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Malaysia\)) denied a report by the *[South China Morning Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_China_Morning_Post)* that it had "bungled" the evacuation of Malaysian nationals from Ukraine by forcing them to travel to Poland in private vehicles to escape the Russian invasion, after reports that the chartered bus scheduled to pick them up failed to arrive. This was followed by allegations that the Malaysian government had dismissed the possibility of a Russian invasion as a "Western narrative".[^cite_note-175][^cite_note-176] At the UN emergency session, Malaysia's representative announced it would vote in favor of the draft resolution condemning the invasion.[^cite_note-unemsession-177] |
| [Maldives](19117.md) | Maldives voted for [United Nations General Assembly Resolution ES-11/1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_ES-11/1), condemning the invasion.[^cite_note-The_Diplomat-70] |
| [Malta](19137.md) | Prime Minister [Robert Abela](62535882.md) said that during the meeting of European leaders Malta had "spoken in favour of peace in Ukraine", adding that doing so "did not compromise Malta's position of neutrality", as Malta's constitution affirms the island as a [neutral state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neutral_country) adhering to a policy of non-alignment.[^cite_note-178] |
| [Marshall Islands](19147.md) | Marshall Islands cosponsored a UN Security Council resolution condemning Russia.[^cite_note-Greenfield-2022-111] |
| [Mauritius](19201.md) | The Mauritian government called for a peaceful resolution of the conflict and joined the call to return to peaceful dialogue between the parties.[^cite_note-179] |
| [Mexico](3966054.md) | The [Secretary of Foreign Affairs of Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretary_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Mexico\)) [Marcelo Ebrard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcelo_Ebrard), on behalf of President [Andrés Manuel López Obrador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andr%C3%A9s_Manuel_L%C3%B3pez_Obrador), issued a statement on Twitter, rejecting and condemning the Russian invasion. He demanded a cessation of hostilities to achieve a peaceful resolution.[^cite_note-180] On 1 March, however, López Obrador announced that Mexico would not participate in economic sanctions against Russia and criticised the overseas censorship of Russian state media.[^cite_note-181] At the [2022 G20 Bali summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_G20_Bali_summit), López Obrador reaffirmed that Mexico would, officially, remain neutral on the conflict.[^cite_note-182] |
| [Moldova](19260.md) | President [Maia Sandu](40796149.md) condemned the act of war, calling it "a blatant breach of international law and of Ukrainian sovereignty and territorial integrity."[^cite_note-183] She added that [Moldova](19260.md) was ready to accept tens of thousands of people fleeing Ukraine after the Russian attack and vowed to keep the borders open to help.[^cite_note-184] |
| [Monaco](19261.md) | [Prince Albert II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albert_II,_Prince_of_Monaco) affirmed his support for Ukraine in a statement: "The Principality reaffirms its support of international laws, and to the sovereignty, the integrity and the independence of states." and "Monaco is at the side of the Ukrainian population and human rights."[^cite_note-185] [Minister of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_State_\(Monaco\)) [Pierre Dartout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre_Dartout) announced his concern and said that Monaco was on the side of the Ukrainian people. He urged the Russians to cease their military operation immediately and for dialogue to resume.[^cite_note-186] The Monaco Royal Palace said in a statement "The Principality has adopted and implemented, without delay, procedures for freezing funds and economic sanctions identical to those taken by most European States."[^cite_note-187] |
| [Montenegro](20760.md) | President [Milo Đukanović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milo_%C4%90ukanovi%C4%87) condemned the Russian invasion, saying it "violates all fundamental principles of international law, undermines European security, and endangers its stability." Deputy Prime Minister [Dritan Abazović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dritan_Abazovi%C4%87) also tweeted that Montenegro stands with NATO and EU partners.[^cite_note-EWB-188] |
| [Nepal](171166.md) | Nepal opposed the invasion and said principles of sovereignty and territorial integrity must be fully respected.[^cite_note-189] |
| [New Zealand](4913064.md) | Prime Minister [Jacinda Ardern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacinda_Ardern) condemned Russia's invasion of Ukraine and called for Russia to immediately withdraw from Ukraine in a bid to avoid a "catastrophic and pointless" loss of life. New Zealand has suspended high-level diplomatic engagements with Russia and introduced travel bans and export controls.[^cite_note-190][^cite_note-191] In early March 2022, the [New Zealand Parliament](340639.md) passed legislation imposing sanctions on Russian elites, organisations and assets deemed complicit in the invasion.[^cite_note-192] In late March, the [New Zealand Defence Force](21359.md) dispatched nine intelligence analysts to assist British and Belgian intelligence gathering work relating to the war in Ukraine.[^cite_note-193] In mid-April 2022, New Zealand dispatched a [C-130 Hercules](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C-130_Hercules) aircraft and 58 military personnel to assist with logistics and transportation.[^cite_note-194]  [Cook Islands](7067.md) – In a statement, the Cook Islands Ministry of Foreign Affairs and Immigration said: "The Cook Islands join with others in condemning Russia's unprovoked invasion of the Ukraine. We call on Russia to end the hostilities and violations of the international rule of law."[^cite_note-195] |
| [Netherlands](21148.md) | Prime Minister [Mark Rutte](4321056.md) condemned Russia's actions in the "strongest possible terms", saying that "one country and one man are responsible", while calling for "maximum sanctions" against Russia.[^cite_note-196] King [Willem-Alexander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willem-Alexander) and Queen [Máxima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_M%C3%A1xima_of_the_Netherlands) stated that their "hearts go out to the people of Ukraine and everyone affected by the violence."[^cite_note-197] The [Dutch royal family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_royal_family) announced it would host six to eight families of refugees from Ukraine from mid-April at [Het Oude Loo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Het_Oude_Loo).[^cite_note-198] |
| [Nigeria](21383.md) | On 24 February 2022, the [Nigerian Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Nigeria\)) describing the invasion as a "surprise". It announced plans for the evacuation of Nigerian citizens from Ukraine. However, it avoided condemning the actions of Russia, and reaffirmed Russia's claims that the attacks in Ukraine had "been confined to military installations".[^cite_note-199] On 26 February 2022, after Foreign Minister [Geoffrey Onyeama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geoffrey_Onyeama) met with Ukrainian Ambassador to Nigeria Kirdoda Valerii, Russian ambassador to Nigeria [Alexey Shebarshin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexey_Shebarshin), and envoys from the [G7](1196634.md) countries, he officially condemned Russia's invasion and urged Russian troops be withdrawn.[^cite_note-200] |
| [North Macedonia](23564616.md) | President [Stevo Pendarovski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stevo_Pendarovski) condemned the Russian invasion as "an attack on the territorial integrity and sovereignty of Ukraine, blatant violation of the basic principles of international law, attack on the democratic order and a threat to the stability of Europe."[^cite_note-201] Prime Minister [Dimitar Kovačevski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dimitar_Kova%C4%8Devski) announced his government's readiness to receive refugees from Ukraine if the situation there deteriorated further.[^cite_note-202] |
| [Norway](21241.md) | Prime Minister [Jonas Gahr Støre](2926932.md) assured that Norway "condemns Russia's military attack on Ukraine in the strongest possible terms".[^cite_note-203] |
| [Palau](23372.md) | Palau participated in the Albanian-American-led Joint Statement following a vote on a UN Security Council resolution on Russia's aggression toward Ukraine.[^cite_note-usun.usmission.gov-155] |
| [Paraguay](23420.md) | Paraguay condemned the invasion.[^cite_note-204] |
| [Papua New Guinea](22943.md) | Papua New Guinea cosponsored a UN Security Council resolution condemning Russia.[^cite_note-Greenfield-2022-111] |
| [Panama](22997.md) | Panama lamented the invasion and stated their support for Ukrainian sovereignty, independence and territorial integrity.[^cite_note-205] |
| [Peru](170691.md) | Foreign Affairs Minister [César Landa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C3%A9sar_Landa) said in a statement: "Faced with the violation of \[the] sovereignty, territory, and integrity of Ukraine, Peru rejects the use of force and states its opinion through the Foreign Affairs Ministry, invoking respect for international law."[^cite_note-206] |
| [Philippines](23440.md) | The Philippines [Department of Foreign Affairs](4718612.md) urged the international community to reaffirm its commitment to peaceful dialogue and "more than words" and cited the UN General Assembly's Manila Declaration on the Peaceful Settlement of International Disputes. Foreign Secretary [Teodoro Locsin Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teodoro_Locsin_Jr.) committed to going to the Ukrainian border to personally oversee the repatriation of Filipinos in Ukraine.[^cite_note-207] The Philippines later said it would vote to condemn the invasion at the emergency special session of the [United Nations General Assembly](31957.md).[^cite_note-208] President [Rodrigo Duterte](7407293.md) appealed to Putin, who is also his personal friend, on 23 May to rein in Russian armed forces following reports of civilians deaths, and to assure safety for those fleeing the area. He acknowledged the conflict was a war against a sovereign nation rather than a "special military operation".[^cite_note-209][^cite_note-210] |
| [Poland](22936.md) | Prime Minister [Mateusz Morawiecki](48505487.md) wrote on Twitter that "We must immediately respond to Russia's criminal aggression on Ukraine Europe and the free world has to stop Putin",[^cite_note-211] and its government announced that the country was "ready to accept migrants and has prepared hospitals and a train to transport Ukrainians wounded in the assault".[^cite_note-212] The [National Broadcasting Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krajowa_Rada_Radiofonii_i_Telewizji) banned the Russian state-controlled television network [RT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RT_\(TV_network\)) on 24 February.[^cite_note-213] |
| [Portugal](23033.md) | Prime Minister [António Costa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ant%C3%B3nio_Costa) "vehemently condemns the military action triggered by Russia today on Ukrainian soil" in a press statement following a meeting with the minister of state and foreign affairs, the minister of defence, and the chief of the general staff.[^cite_note-214] |
| [Romania](25445.md) | President [Klaus Iohannis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klaus_Iohannis) condemned Russia's military aggression against Ukraine via Twitter. He said that "Romania, together with the entire international democratic community, strongly rejects this irresponsible behaviour that undermines the foundations of international relations and the current order of international law", and that "the Russian Federation has once again shown that it is not is interested in the constructive and responsible dialogue offered by the European and Euro-Atlantic community" and that Romanian citizens should leave Ukraine as soon as possible.[^cite_note-215] |
| [San Marino](27248.md) | The [San Marino Secretary for Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Marino_Secretary_for_Foreign_Affairs) issued a statement saying that "the military escalation in Ukraine is a great wound for all the people and nations who strongly believe in the values of peace and strongly condemn war" and "the San Marino institutions and government are in deep shock at this time".[^cite_note-216] |
| [Saint Lucia](27208.md) | Saint Lucia expressed concern about the invasion and supported the [CARICOM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caribbean_Community) statement condemning Russia.[^cite_note-217] |
| [Samoa](27238.md) | Samoa's permanent representative to the United Nations, Fatumanava-o-Upolu III Pa'olelei Luteru, described the invasion as "unprovoked" and stated that "Samoa is greatly concerned by the invasion of Ukraine by the Russian Federation. Such action is a clear violation of the territorial integrity and sovereignty." He also called for Russia and Ukraine to engage in peace negotiations.[^cite_note-218] |
| [Sierra Leone](27308.md) | President [Julius Maada Bio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julius_Maada_Bio) urged an end to the war in Ukraine "for the sake of humanity", stating that he believes "even those who sympathise with [Russia](25391.md) are in favour of stopping this war".[^cite_note-219] Amid his 2023 candidacy for reelection, Bio indicated that he hopes diplomacy led by African leaders can help broker an end to the war.[^cite_note-220] |
| [Singapore](27318.md) | [Singapore](27318.md)'s [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](2552683.md) said Singapore "strongly condemns any unprovoked invasion of a sovereign country under any pretext," and that "the sovereignty, independence and territorial integrity of Ukraine must be respected."[^cite_note-221] "All parties concerned should continue to pursue dialogue, including diplomatic means, towards a peaceful settlement of the dispute, in accordance with international law, and avoid activities that will further raise tensions in the region."[^cite_note-222] Local news outlets reported that Singapore might join international sanctions on Russia, and was unlikely to take a direct economic hit from export curbs on Russia.[^cite_note-223] On 28 February 2022, Singapore announced sanctions against Russia, export controls on items "that can be used directly as weapons in Ukraine to inflict harm or to subjugate the Ukrainians" and moved to "block certain Russian banks and financial transactions connected to Russia". The move was a first by a Southeast Asian country and a departure from the Association of Southeast Asian Nations' refusal to condemn the invasion.[^cite_note-224][^cite_note-225] |
| [Slovakia](26830.md) | Prime Minister [Eduard Heger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eduard_Heger) stated that "The Russian imperialism has been restored in front of our eyes in its aggressive, militant form" and about Russian president Vladimir Putin added "All victims of this war will be his victims and he will be responsible for them in the eyes of the global public."[^cite_note-226] On 24 February 2022, [Bratislava Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bratislava_Castle) and the seat of President of Slovakia [Grassalkovich Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grassalkovich_Palace) in Bratislava was light up in blue and yellow in solidarity with Ukraine.[^cite_note-227] Slovakia also provided Ukraine with an [S-300](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S-300_missile_system) air defense system.[^cite_note-228] |
| [Slovenia](27338.md) | Prime Minister [Janez Janša](1038986.md) condemned Russia's "unprecedented military aggression against Ukraine" and demanded that Russia immediately withdraw its military and fully respect Ukraine's territorial integrity, reaffirming Slovenia's support of Ukraine.[^cite_note-229] The national flag of Ukraine was hung from the Slovenian Parliament in [Ljubljana](42374.md) as a symbol of solidarity and brotherhood between the two countries.[^cite_note-230] |
| [South Korea](27019.md) | President [Moon Jae-in](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon_Jae-in) said that South Korea would join sanctions against Russia. He said: "The use of armed forces causing human casualties cannot be justified under any circumstances".[^cite_note-231] The Ministry of Foreign Affairs said: "The Korean government strongly condemns Russia's armed invasion against Ukraine as a violation of the principles of the UN Charter" and "the Korean government will support and join the international community's efforts, including economic sanctions, to curb armed invasion".[^cite_note-232] On 1 March, South Korea announced it would stop all transactions with seven main Russian banks and their affiliates, and restrict the purchase of Russian treasury bonds.[^cite_note-233][^cite_note-234] In April 2022, South Korea turned down Ukraine's request for ground-based systems targeting aircraft and ballistic missiles, citing its "principled stance" of not providing lethal hardware. At the time, Ukraine had already been provided with approximately 25,000 anti-air and 60,000 anti-tank systems by the U.S. and its allies.[^cite_note-235] |
| [Spain](26667.md) | Prime Minister [Pedro Sánchez](43297512.md) condemned the "intolerable military actions of the Russian government in Ukrainian soil" on Twitter after holding a meeting of the [Spanish National Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Security_Council_\(Spain\)) presided over by [King Felipe VI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felipe_VI_of_Spain).[^cite_note-236] Minister of Foreign Affairs [José Manuel Albares](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Manuel_Albares) called the attack "unjustifiable" and a "blatant violation of international law", and announced that Spain was coordinating with EU partners and NATO allies.[^cite_note-237] Minister of Defence [Margarita Robles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margarita_Robles) asked for "really severe" sanctions and called Russian actions "of an extraordinary gravity" but noted that there would be no NATO troops on Ukrainian soil as the country "is not a NATO member".[^cite_note-238] On 27 February, Spanish Defence Minister Margarita Robles announced the shipment of 20 tons of defense military equipment to Ukraine[^cite_note-239] and advanced the request to send the [frigate Blas de Lezo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_frigate_Blas_de_Lezo_\(F103\)) in conjunction with a NATO mission.[^cite_note-240] |
| [Suriname](26828.md) | Suriname condemned the invasion.[^cite_note-241] |
| [Sweden](5058739.md) | Prime Minister [Magdalena Andersson](41178429.md) stated that "Sweden condemns in the strongest terms Russia's ongoing invasion of Ukraine. Russia's acts are also an attack on the European security order. It will be met by a united and robust response in solidarity with Ukraine. Russia alone is responsible for human suffering."[^cite_note-242] Andersson announced that Sweden would apply for NATO membership on 16 May, in coordination with neighboring [Finland's application](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finland%E2%80%93NATO_relations).[^cite_note-243] |
| [Switzerland](26748.md) | The Swiss foreign ministry called Russia's actions an "invasion" and a "gross violation of international law", while Swiss President [Ignazio Cassis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ignazio_Cassis) announced that the country would support EU sanctions on travel and finance but would still not impose sanctions of its own.[^cite_note-244] However, the [Swiss Federal Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swiss_Federal_Council) reversed course on 28 February, announcing that Switzerland was imposing the same sanctions on Russian assets as the European Union. Exempt from these sanctions were payments for energy raw materials. According to Cassis, the decision was unprecedented but consistent with [Swiss neutrality](15380880.md).[^cite_note-245] The Ukraine Recovery International Conference took place in [Lugano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lugano), [Switzerland](26748.md) on 5 July 2022, to finance the rebuilding of the war-torn nation.[^cite_note-246] |
| [Thailand](30128.md) | Thailand's [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](20529358.md) expressed "deep concern" and said that it supported "peaceful settlement to the situation through dialogue."[^cite_note-247] |
| [Timor-Leste](19159399.md) | Timor-Leste was one of 87 signatories to the UN letter condemning Russia's invasion of Ukraine.[^cite_note-Axios-71] |
| [Trinidad and Tobago](3565457.md) | Foreign Minister Amery Browne condemned the invasion.[^cite_note-248] |
| [Tunisia](30188.md) | Tunisia called on all parties to work on settling the conflict through peaceful means, expressed "great concern" over the aggravation of tension in the region."[^cite_note-249] Tunisia called on its nationals in Ukraine not to leave the country without prior coordination.[^cite_note-250] |
| [United Kingdom](31717.md) | [The Queen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_II) made a "generous" donation to the Disasters Emergency Committee (DEC) Ukraine Humanitarian Appeal.[^cite_note-Royal-Donations-251] Prime Minister [Boris Johnson](19065069.md) said that he was "appalled by the horrific events in Ukraine" and "President Putin has chosen a path of bloodshed and destruction by launching this unprovoked attack".[^cite_note-252] During a visit to [Southend-on-Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southend-on-Sea), the [Prince of Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles,_Prince_of_Wales) condemned the Russian invasion, stating: "What we saw in the [terrible tragedy in Southend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murder_of_David_Amess) was an attack on democracy, on an open society, on freedom itself. We are seeing those same values under attack today in Ukraine in the most unconscionable way. In the stand we take here, we are in solidarity with all those who are resisting brutal aggression."[^cite_note-253] His wife, the [Duchess of Cornwall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camilla,_Duchess_of_Cornwall), made a "substantial" donation to the *[Daily Mail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daily_Mail)*'s refugee campaign.[^cite_note-Royal-Donations-251] The [royal household](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Households_of_the_United_Kingdom#Household_of_the_Prince_and_Princess_of_Wales) of the [Duke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William,_Prince_of_Wales) and [Duchess of Cambridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catherine,_Princess_of_Wales) stated on Twitter: "In October 2020 we had the privilege to meet President Zelenskyy and the First Lady to learn of their hope and optimism for Ukraine's future. Today we stand with the President and all of Ukraine's people as they bravely fight for that future."[^cite_note-254] The couple also made a private donation and were thanked by President Zelenskyy for their message of support.[^cite_note-Royal-Donations-251] Secretary of State for Defence [Ben Wallace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Wallace_\(politician\)) described Russia's actions as "naked aggression against a democratic country".[^cite_note-255]  [Gibraltar](7607314.md) – "Today's actions by Russia, launching a full-scale invasion of a sovereign, democratic nation without any provocation or reasonable excuse, is nothing short of appalling. None of us expected to witness such unforgivable aggression in Europe in our lifetimes. Gibraltar therefore joins with the Prime Minister of the United Kingdom, leaders of other nations and people around the world in condemning this action in the most strident terms", said Chief Minister [Fabian Picardo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fabian_Picardo).[^cite_note-256] Picardo called for a ban of the Russian state-controlled television network RT on 25 February; television providers in Gibraltar agreed to suspend broadcasts of RT.[^cite_note-257]  [Guernsey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guernsey) – "Guernsey follows the UK's sanctions regime and UK foreign policy and will continue to do so. Some of the announced measures will apply automatically and this has already been communicated to the industry. Some of the other sanctions announced yesterday may require new legislation in the UK and if so, the States will move in step with the UK to ensure that all new sanctions will apply and be enforced uniformly."[^cite_note-258]  [Isle of Man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isle_of_Man) – The Isle of Man government is in close contact with the UK government and will remain so regarding the unfolding situation in Ukraine. "We will take action in line with the UK government."[^cite_note-259] It since closed its airspace and ports to Russian airplanes and ships, and extended the UK's sanctions to automatically apply on the Isle of Man. The chief minister [Alfred Cannan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfred_Cannan) condemned the actions of the Russian President.[^cite_note-260]  [Jersey](15693.md) – The Deputy (Acting) Chief Minister, Senator [Lyndon Farnham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyndon_Farnham), issued the following statement: "We are fully alongside the United Kingdom in its condemnation of this aggressive action, and we will act promptly in line with the UK's response. The UK is ultimately responsible for our foreign relations as a matter of international law, and we implement both U.K. and UN sanctions.[^cite_note-261] |
| [United States](3434750.md) | President [Joe Biden](145422.md) released a statement condemning the Russian invasion as "unprovoked and unjustified" and accused Putin of starting a "premeditated war that will bring a catastrophic loss of life and human suffering".[^cite_note-262] Biden stated the US would not dispatch its own forces to protect Ukraine, however, Biden did authorize sanctions directly targeting Putin and Russian Foreign Minister [Sergey Lavrov](541346.md).[^cite_note-263][^cite_note-264] In the [2022 State of the Union Address](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_State_of_the_Union_Address), Biden announced that all US airspace would be closed to all Russian aircraft.[^cite_note-265] Biden additionally condemned [Russian oligarchs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_oligarch) who supported Putin, stating that "We are joining with our European allies to find and seize your yachts, your luxury apartments, your private jets. We are coming for your ill-begotten gains."[^cite_note-266] Vice President [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris) threatened to increase sanctions against Russia at the [Munich Security Conference](1464620.md): "Let me be clear, I can say with absolute certainty: If Russia further invades Ukraine, the United States, together with our allies and partners, will impose significant and unprecedented economic costs."[^cite_note-267] House speaker [Nancy Pelosi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nancy_Pelosi) vowed the House would pass as much funding as needed to support the Ukrainian government.[^cite_note-268] Senate Majority Leader [Chuck Schumer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuck_Schumer) said that "there's a Holocaust going on" in Ukraine.[^cite_note-269] |
| [Uruguay](31843.md) | President [Luis Lacalle Pou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Lacalle_Pou) condemned Russia's "actions contrary to international law", and declared that Uruguay "is a country that is always committed to peace", for which he encouraged the return of negotiations for a "civilised resolution" of the conflict.[^cite_note-270] In an official statement from the [Ministry of Foreign Relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Relations_\(Uruguay\)), the government stated that the principles of the Charter of the United Nations were "notoriously violated" after the Russian military attacks; and announced that four Uruguayan citizens were evacuated from Ukraine to Cyprus, and that it was monitoring the situation, attending to the situation of its "compatriots" in order to provide them with the proper assistance.[^cite_note-271][^cite_note-272] |

#### Governments in exile

| State | Notes |
| --- | --- |
| [Afghanistan](737.md) | [Chargé d'Affaires of Afghanistan's Permanent Mission to the United Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permanent_Representative_of_Afghanistan_to_the_United_Nations) [Naseer Ahmad Faiq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naseer_Faiq), who represents the country's internationally recognized former government, the [Islamic Republic of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Republic_of_Afghanistan), voted for [United Nations General Assembly Resolution ES-11/1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_ES-11/1), condemning the invasion.[^cite_note-The_Diplomat-70] |
| [Myanmar](19457.md) | The Minister of International Cooperation of the [exiled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_in_exile) [National Unity Government of Myanmar](67413816.md), [Dr. Sasa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sasa_\(politician\)), said "The unprovoked, unjustified attacks on Ukraine by Russia are inexcusable and unacceptable."[^cite_note-273] |

#### Non UN members

| State | Notes |
| --- | --- |
| [Kosovo](17391.md) | Prime Minister [Albin Kurti](4774301.md) condemned the Russian invasion as "the largest military aggression, not only in Europe, since the end of the Second World War", stating "we stand in solidarity with the people of Ukraine and stand together with the EU, NATO, the US, and the UK for state sovereignty, territorial integrity, the country's independence and the right of self-determination for the people of Ukraine", alongside condemnations by the Kosovar parliament. Meanwhile, President [Vjosa Osmani](61979540.md) said that the people of Kosovo supported Ukrainians "as they face an unprovoked war as a result of Russian aggression", stating on Twitter that "we will work with our allies. ... Russian hegemony will not triumph. Freedom and democracy will win".[^cite_note-prishtinainsight.com-41] A joint statement by Kosovo's president, prime minister and senior ministers furthermore condemned attempts to draw parallels to Kosovo's own declaration of independence from Serbia: "Dictator Putin's effort to refer to the Kosovo case and draw parallel are totally unstable, abusive and an attempt to camouflage the lack of any base or reason for the barbarous attack of its forces against a sovereign state."[^cite_note-274] |
| [Northern Cyprus](271409.md)[^cite_note-275] | The Ministry of Foreign Affairs strongly recommended all Turkish Cypriots in Ukraine to take all possible safety measures as well as services for those looking to leave Ukraine.[^cite_note-276] |
| [Taiwan](25734.md)[^cite_note-277] | [President](61155.md) [Tsai Ing-wen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsai_Ing-wen) stated "our government condemns Russia's violation of Ukraine's sovereignty and urges all parties to continue to resolve the disputes through peaceful and rational means."[^cite_note-278] The [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Taiwan\)) announced that Taiwan would join international sanctions against Russia and expressed regret over Russia's decision to "use force and intimidation in bullying others instead of resolving disputes through peaceful diplomatic negotiations."[^cite_note-279] Taiwan moved to block Russian banks from the SWIFT system and sent 27 tonnes of military aid. Later, President Tsai Ing-wen, [Vice President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vice_President_of_the_Republic_of_China) [William Lai](22609914.md) and [Premier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Premier_of_the_Republic_of_China) [Su Tseng-chang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Su_Tseng-chang) announced they would each donate one month of salary towards humanitarian aid for Ukraine.[^cite_note-280] |

### Leans to Ukraine

While these countries have taken some neutral positions, in general they have been described as being supportive of Ukraine.

| State | Notes |
| --- | --- |
