| [Bangladesh](3454.md) | Foreign Minister [AK Abdul Momen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AK_Abdul_Momen) called for the conflict to be peacefully resolved in line with the Charter of the United Nations.[^cite_note-281] Prime Minister [Sheikh Hasina](623439.md) asked all concerned persons to observe the Russia-Ukraine war situation.[^cite_note-282] Russia is building a megaproject in Bangladesh, the [Rooppur Nuclear Power Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rooppur_Nuclear_Power_Plant). Russia provides all the funding and logistics for this project, but experts suspect that the war could disrupt the work. Even though 77% of the construction work is already done, the war situation could prolong the rest of the construction. The [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union), of which Russia and Ukraine were both part, helped Bangladesh gain independence from Pakistan in the [1971 Bangladesh Liberation War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_Liberation_War), by providing extensive military and diplomatic assistance.[^cite_note-283] The neutral stance the country took in the invasion was believed to be related to concerns that Bangladesh itself did not want to be threatened by larger [nuclear armed](21785.md) neighbours.[^cite_note-284] A year after the invasion of Ukraine, *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)* changed Bangladesh from "Neutral" to "West leaning".[^cite_note-Who_are_Russia's_supporters-72] In 2023, Bangladesh for the first time condemned Russia and stated it's 'violation of international law'.[^cite_note-285] |
| [Brazil](3383.md) | Putin with Brazilian President [Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva](99328.md) in Moscow, Russia, 9 May 2025 President [Jair Bolsonaro](46603496.md) declined to condemn the Russian invasion of Ukraine, while departing from his government's official stance at the United Nations to say Brazil would remain neutral.[^cite_note-286] Brazil supported a resolution condemning Russia's invasion of Ukraine at a United Nations Security Council meeting on 25 February.[^cite_note-287] Vice-president [Hamilton Mourão](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamilton_Mour%C3%A3o) suggested use of force against Russia in the context of military crisis.[^cite_note-288] However, on 27 February, President Jair Bolsonaro said that he would not condemn the invasion and that Brazil would remain neutral.[^cite_note-289] In 2023, Brazilian President [Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva](99328.md) condemned the invasion, but suggested Ukraine could "give up Crimea" in exchange for [peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Russia%E2%80%93Ukraine_peace_negotiations) and Russia's withdrawal from the Ukrainian territory it occupied after February 2022, which Ukraine rejected.[^cite_note-290][^cite_note-291] |
| [Bosnia and Herzegovina](3463.md) | Croat and Bosniak members of the tripartite presidency, [Željko Komšić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%BDeljko_Kom%C5%A1i%C4%87) and [Šefik Džaferović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0efik_D%C5%BEaferovi%C4%87), issued separate statements condemning the Russian invasion, with Komšić saying that Bosnia would support Ukraine.[^cite_note-prishtinainsight.com-41] The Serb member [Milorad Dodik](4218129.md) did not, instead stating that Bosnia and Herzegovina was neutral. He'd said the previous day that events showed it was good Bosnia and Herzegovina had not joined NATO, and that the country would not support sanctions.[^cite_note-EWB-188] Foreign Minister [Bisera Turković](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bisera_Turkovi%C4%87) said: "Bosnia and Herzegovina remains firmly committed to Ukraine's sovereignty and territorial integrity, and we call for an immediate end to fighting and shelling! OSCE principles, security and international law are under attack today. Hostility and suffering of innocent civilians must end immediately". Turković called on Russia and Belarus to refrain from using force in the interests of peace, and regional and global stability.[^cite_note-292] |
| [Hungary](13275.md) | President [János Áder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%A1nos_%C3%81der) said "Hungary was also forced to endure a [similar (invasion) in 1956](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarian_Revolution_of_1956)". Áder added that "we \[Hungary] have done everything we could to urge the leadership in Moscow to remain level-headed and exercise restraint. Unfortunately, we could not succeed because, as it has now become clear, Russia was carrying out a premeditated plan which was followed by the conscious deception of its international partners".[^cite_note-293] Prime Minister [Viktor Orbán](750582.md) stated that "together with our EU and NATO allies, we condemn Russia's military attack", adding that sending either troops or military equipment to Ukraine was "out of the question, though we will, of course, provide humanitarian aid".[^cite_note-294] A year after the invasion of Ukraine, *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)* downgraded Hungary from "Condemns Russia" to "West leaning".[^cite_note-Who_are_Russia's_supporters-72] |
| [Israel](9282173.md) | Israeli Prime Minister [Naftali Bennett](39754840.md) called for a halt to the violence and pledged humanitarian aid to Ukraine, also offering to mediate and broker a ceasefire between Russia and Ukraine.[^cite_note-295][^cite_note-296] Foreign Affairs Minister and Alternate Prime Minister [Yair Lapid](2448072.md) called the attack "a grave violation of the international order. Israel condemns the attack, and is ready and prepared to provide humanitarian assistance to the citizens of Ukraine. Israel is a country that has experienced wars, and war is not the way to resolve conflicts."[^cite_note-297][^cite_note-298][^cite_note-299] On 27 February 2022, Israeli Interior Minister [Ayelet Shaked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayelet_Shaked) declared a two-month visa extension for all Ukrainian tourists in Israel, so they could receive temporary asylum from the war in Ukraine.[^cite_note-300] On 8 March 2022, Shaked announced an official policy of hosting Israel's Ukrainian refugees who were not eligible for [aliyah](351513.md). All 16,000 Ukrainians in Israel before the invasion who were not citizens would receive an automatic three-month extension of their residence visa in Israel. If the war continued beyond that, they would be able to stay in Israel automatically and even receive work permits and the right to Israeli medical insurance. An Israeli pension for non-Israeli Ukrainian adults who would not be able to work in the future was also being considered. It also decided to cancel the economic guarantee requirement for Israeli citizens who hosted Ukrainians in their homes, and opened an additional quota of 5,000 Ukrainian refugees. Any Ukrainian would be able to submit an online application on the Foreign Ministry website and until the quota was filled, in the absence of other issues, would be able to come to Israel temporarily. Israeli citizens would be able to apply to invite Ukrainian citizens, up to one nuclear family per applicant, and these would receive as much priority as possible. In total, Israel planned to host 25,000 refugees for the duration of the war.[^cite_note-301] On 13 March, Shaked announced that anyone with a relative in Israel would be exempt from the quota and still would receive legal refugee status. On 3 July 2022, the Israeli Supreme Court abolished the quotas on Ukrainian refugees and allowed unlimited entry of refugees into Israel. The President of Ukraine welcomed the decision as a sign of "a true, developed democracy".[^cite_note-302] Despite voting to condemn Russia's invasion of Ukraine, Israel has offered to be a mediator in the crisis.[^cite_note-303] |
| [Jordan](7515964.md) | The Minister of Foreign Affairs of Jordan urged the international community and the parties to spare no effort to promote restraint and de-escalation, and called for peaceful settlement of the conflict through dialogue to restore security and stability in the region.[^cite_note-304] Jordan has been classified as a "West leaning" state on the Russia-Ukraine crisis according to *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)*.[^cite_note-Who_are_Russia's_supporters-72] |
| [Saint Vincent and the Grenadines](27228.md) | At the UN emergency session, ambassador [Inga Rhonda King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inga_Rhonda_King) stated that the country "unequivocally insists that the Russian Federation cease its military operations and immediately withdraw (its) forces from Ukraine."[^cite_note-305] |
| [São Tomé and Príncipe](20026306.md) | President of São Tomé and Príncipe [Carlos Vila Nova](68450389.md) said that the country "is not in favor of war" in Ukraine and hopes that the parties can still talk "to resolve disputes".[^cite_note-306] |
| [Serbia](29265.md) | While condemning the invasion of Ukraine, Serbia refused to back sanctions against Russia.[^cite_note-307] Serbia respected Ukraine's territorial integrity and considered Russia's action against it "wrong", but would not impose sanctions against Moscow, Serbian President [Aleksandar Vučić](13349267.md) said on 25 February. Vučić said the National Security Council concluded the Republic of Serbia considers "very wrong the violation of territorial integrity of a number of countries including Ukraine."[^cite_note-308][^cite_note-309] Vučić also said that he would condemn Russia's recognition of the independence of separatist regions in eastern Ukraine only if Zelenskyy condemned the [NATO bombing of Yugoslavia](322473.md) in 1999 on public television.[^cite_note-310] Serbian Foreign Minister [Nikola Selaković](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikola_Selakovi%C4%87) said that "Russia was Serbia's biggest support in its battle to preserve its territorial integrity and sovereignty and avoid the stigmatisation of the entire Serb people." Serbian Interior Minister [Aleksandar Vulin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleksandar_Vulin) stressed that Serbia would not join NATO: "While Aleksandar Vučić is the head of the state, you should have no dilemma as to what our attitude toward the NATO alliance is."[^cite_note-Euractiv-2022-02-25-311] While there was no agreement on sanctions on Russia, Serbia nevertheless expressed regrets over events, describing both Russia and Ukraine as friendly states and underlining full support for the territorial integrity of Ukraine.[^cite_note-312][^cite_note-313] Serbian President [Aleksandar Vučić](13349267.md) with Russian President [Vladimir Putin](32817.md), 2 September 2025 After the military assault began, some Serbian pro-government newspapers hailed Russia's invasion of Ukraine, praising that Russia "overran" Ukraine, Moscow's troops "reached Kyiv in a day" and that the Russian attack on Ukraine was a "response to NATO threats".[^cite_note-314] Serbian Parliament Speaker [Ivica Dačić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivica_Da%C4%8Di%C4%87) said that Serbia would never impose sanctions on its friend Russia, despite the pressure that it faced. Dačić also added that "Russia is our most influential ally for preserving our territorial integrity and sovereignty in Kosovo."[^cite_note-Euractiv-2022-02-25-311] The initial weak response by the government was criticised by some commentators in the country and the region.[^cite_note-315][^cite_note-316] The Serbian organization [Women in Black](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women_in_Black) organised anti-war demonstrations in [Belgrade](55904.md), and the [Serbian Orthodox Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_Orthodox_Church) organised a collection of humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-317][^cite_note-318] On 2 March Serbia voted 'yes' to the UN's resolution condemning the Russian attack on Ukraine.[^cite_note-319] On 16 May Serbia signed the "Tirana declaration" and deplored in the strongest terms the illegal and unjustified aggression by the Russian Federation against Ukraine in violation of Article 2(4) of the UN Charter.[^cite_note-320] *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)* classifies Serbia as "neutral".[^cite_note-Who_are_Russia's_supporters-72] |
| [Turkey](11125639.md) | Putin with Turkish President [Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan) at the [SCO Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Tianjin_SCO_summit) in Tianjin, China, 1 September 2025 Turkish President [Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan) and the [Turkish Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs) said that Russian actions were "unacceptable" and condemned Russia for its "grave violation of international law", while reiterating "support for Ukraine's sovereignty and territorial integrity".[^cite_note-321] A commercial Turkish-owned vessel was hit by a Russian bomb off the coast of [Odesa](55545.md). Turkish authorities said there were no casualties and the ship safely arrived in the territorial waters of [Romania](25445.md), a NATO ally.[^cite_note-322] On 27 February, Turkish Foreign Minister [Mevlüt Çavuşoğlu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mevl%C3%BCt_%C3%87avu%C5%9Fo%C4%9Flu) referred to the invasion as a "war", bringing the conflict under the 1936 [Montreux Convention Regarding the Regime of the Straits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montreux_Convention_Regarding_the_Regime_of_the_Straits) and permitting Turkey to prohibit Russian warships in the [Bosporus](3705.md) and [Dardanelles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dardanelles).[^cite_note-323] On 28 February, Erdoğan publicly confirmed the closure, and pledged to maintain relations with both Ukraine and Russia.[^cite_note-324] A year after the invasion of Ukraine, *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)* downgraded Turkey from "West leaning" to "neutral".[^cite_note-Who_are_Russia's_supporters-72] |

### Neutral countries

| State | Notes |
| --- | --- |
| [Afghanistan](737.md) | On 25 February 2022 the [Taliban](30635.md) called for "resolving the crisis through dialogue and peaceful means."[^cite_note-325] |
| [Angola](701.md) | The Angolan government urged a ceasefire and warned that the conflict, in addition to causing human and material damage, caused "a climate of tension between the two countries, with international proportions". The Angolan Ministry of Foreign Affairs said that "the parties must strive for the peaceful resolution of the conflict."[^cite_note-326] |
| [Armenia](10918072.md) | The Armenian MFA spokesman on 23 February expressed "hope that the existing problems between the two friendly states would be resolved through diplomatic dialogue", and declared Armenia's readiness to accept refugees.[^cite_note-327][^cite_note-328] On 1 June 2023, during an interview, [prime minister of Armenia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_minister_of_Armenia) [Nikol Pashinyan](17897961.md) stated, "Armenia is not an ally of Russia in the war in Ukraine."[^cite_note-329] On 7 September 2023 [Anna Hakobyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna_Hakobyan), the wife of Pashinyan, met with [Ukrainian President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_President) [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) and his wife [Olena Zelenska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olena_Zelenska) in [Kyiv](585629.md), Ukraine.[^cite_note-pashinyans-wife-to-ukraineSU-330] She traveled to Kyiv at the invitation of the [First Lady of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Lady_of_Ukraine) to participate in the "Summit of First Ladies and Gentlemen."[^cite_note-pashinyans-wife-to-ukraineSU-330] Hakobyan personally handed over 1,000+ smartphones, tablets, and laptops for primary schoolchildren at the [Ministry of Education and Science of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Education_and_Science_of_Ukraine) in Kyiv.[^cite_note-Hakobyan7418899SU-331] This was Armenia's first delivery of [humanitarian aid for Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_humanitarian_aid_to_Ukraine_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War) since the Russian invasion started in February 2022.[^cite_note-Hakobyan7418899SU-331] In June 2024, Armenia participated in the [Ukraine peace summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_2024_Ukraine_peace_summit).[^cite_note-332] |
| [Azerbaijan](746.md) | The president of [Azerbaijan](746.md) [Ilham Aliyev](22765442.md) expressed his support for territorial integrity of Ukraine, and urged his Ukrainian counterparts to "never to agree to the violation of your territorial integrity".[^cite_note-333][^cite_note-334][^cite_note-335] Ilham Aliyev also offered to organize talks between Ukraine and Russia,[^cite_note-336] instructed Azerbaijan's State Oil Company [SOCAR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SOCAR) to provide free fuel to Ukraine's ambulances and [State Emergency Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Emergency_Service_of_Ukraine)[^cite_note-337] and sent humanitarian aid to Ukraine.[^cite_note-338][^cite_note-339] The government of Azerbaijan has taken a neutral position on the conflict, and has maintained strong cooperation with both Moscow and Kyiv.[^cite_note-340] |
| [Bolivia](3462.md) | The [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Bolivia\)) stated that, as "a pacifist state", the Bolivian government urged Russia and Ukraine to avoid the use of force and seek diplomatic de-escalation "within the framework of International Law and the Charter of the United Nations". It did not explicitly condemn the Russian invasion, instead citing a "lack of dialogue and understanding" as causing further escalation in the conflict.[^cite_note-341] Two days later, on 28 February, before the UN General Assembly, Bolivia rejected the "invasion and unilateral actions" of Russia.[^cite_note-342] Nonetheless, Bolivia has consistently abstained from UN and OAS motions condemning Russia or demanding a cessation of hostilities and voted against expelling Russia from the [United Nations Human Rights Council](635790.md).[^cite_note-343][^cite_note-344][^cite_note-345] A year after the invasion of Ukraine, [The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist) downgraded Bolivia from "neutral" to "Russia leaning".[^cite_note-Who_are_Russia's_supporters-72] |
| [El Salvador](9356.md) | The [Nayib Bukele](46452145.md) administration has taken neutrality as its stance on the Russia-Ukraine conflict.[^cite_note-346] |
| [Egypt](8087628.md) | Prime Minister [Mostafa Madbouly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mostafa_Madbouly) expressed his hope the situation would soon be resolved.[^cite_note-347] |
| [Honduras](13394.md) | Honduras condemned the Russian invasion.[^cite_note-348] In December 2023, Honduras voted against condemning Russia at the [United Nations](31769.md).[^cite_note-auto-349] |
| [India](14533.md) | Russian President Vladimir Putin shaking hands Indian Prime Minister Narendra Modi at the 16th BRICS Summit in 2024, in front of the Indian and Russian flags.Russian President [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) with Indian Prime Minister [Narendra Modi](444222.md) at the [16th BRICS Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/16th_BRICS_summit) on 22 October 2024.Prime Minister [Narendra Modi](444222.md) appealed for an immediate cessation of violence in a phone call to Putin on 24 February. He also spoke with Zelenskyy on 26 February without ascribing responsibility for the violence.[^cite_note-350] The Indian government refrained from taking a stand or criticizing Russia.[^cite_note-351] India was also worked on trading with Russia using [rupees](490783.md) to circumvent sanctions.[^cite_note-352] India is the only member of the [Quadrilateral Security Dialogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quadrilateral_Security_Dialogue) not to ban Russian exports. The Ukrainian ambassador to India, Igor Polikha, told reporters in India that he was "deeply dissatisfied" with India's position.[^cite_note-353] India also abstained from the United Nations Security Council resolution condemning the Russian invasion.[^cite_note-354] Many Western capitals criticised India for its refusal to condemn the invasion.[^cite_note-355] UK Prime Minister [Boris Johnson](19065069.md)[^cite_note-visit_cancelled-356] and Prime Minister of Japan [Fumio Kishida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fumio_Kishida)[^cite_note-357] contacted Modi and tried to persuade him to condemn the invasion. The UK also expressed concern over India's rupee-ruble trade arrangements.[^cite_note-visit_cancelled-356] Foreign Minister S Jaishankar said that India would pursue an independent foreign policy and consider options in the current market to meet its energy needs. He added that imports from Russia were just a fraction of India's overall imports.[^cite_note-358] |
| [Kazakhstan](16642.md) | Kazakh President [Kassym-Jomart Tokayev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kassym-Jomart_Tokayev) and Russian President Vladimir Putin in Omsk, Russia, 25 July 2026Kazakhstan, a neighbour and ally of Russia, reportedly denied a request for its troops to join Russian forces in the invasion. The former Soviet republic also said it would not be recognizing the Russia-created breakaway republics, the Luhansk People's Republic and the Donetsk People's Republic, [NBC News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBC_News) reported.[^cite_note-359] The [Kazakh defense ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Defense_\(Kazakhstan\)) insisted the matter had not even come up. A statement issued on its Telegram channel on 27 February said: "A request to send Kazakh military to Ukraine or any other country hasn't been received and, correspondingly, hasn't been considered."[^cite_note-360] Kazakhstan avoided criticising Russia's move to invade a fellow ex-Soviet republic however.[^cite_note-361] President of Kazakhstan [Kassym-Jomart Tokayev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kassym-Jomart_Tokayev) stated that his country would not recognize Luhansk and Donetsk Republics, which he considers to be quasi-states, because if the right of nations to self-determination was realized on the entire globe, over 500 or 600 states would emerge in the world instead of present 193, and it would result in chaos.[^cite_note-362] On 25 July 2026, during a meeting with Russian President Putin in [Omsk](62593.md), Kazakh President Tokayev called for the Russo-Ukrainian war to be “frozen” and for [negotiations](70255183.md) to resume on the basis of what he described as the “Istanbul Formula 2.0”. He expressed concern over the [continued loss of life](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casualties_of_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war) and said that the conflict should be brought to an end.[^cite_note-363][^cite_note-364] |
| [Kyrgyzstan](170131.md) | The Foreign Ministry of Kyrgyzstan called on Russia and Ukraine to negotiate. "We're following the development of the situation in Ukraine with dismay and concern. Proceeding from our historically friendly relations with the peoples of Russia and Ukraine, we hope that the parties will achieve peace promptly, including by means of creating new formats and mechanisms of the conflict settlement at the negotiating table to prevent further casualties and damage," the [Kyrgyz Foreign Ministry](18387673.md) said on Friday.[^cite_note-365] President [Sadyr Japarov](65513403.md) commented that the invasion may have been necessary "to protect the peaceful population of the territories of Donbas." That prompted Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelensky to recall Ukraine's ambassador to Kyrgyzstan to protest the country's statement "justifying the aggression against Ukraine."[^cite_note-366] |
| [Mongolia](19271.md) | Putin and Mongolian President [Ukhnaagiin Khürelsükh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukhnaagiin_Kh%C3%BCrels%C3%BCkh) in Ulaanbaatar, Mongolia, 3 September 2024 [Battsetseg Batmunkh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battsetseg_Batmunkh), Mongolia's foreign minister, said the government was observing events with concern. She noted that "if the situation continued to escalate it may contribute negatively to world peace and stability", and called on the parties to quickly negotiate a ceasefire.[^cite_note-367] In advance of a [visit by Putin to Mongolia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongolia%E2%80%93Russia_relations) on 3 September 2024, the ICC stated that Mongolia was obligated to place Putin under arrest, due to [Mongolia](19271.md) being a signatory of the Rome Statute of the ICC.[^cite_note-368] After failure to make the arrest, Mongolia was described by Ukraine as complicit in Putin's war crimes.[^cite_note-369][^cite_note-370] |
| [Morocco](19291.md) | Morocco's Foreign Ministry released a statement reiterating its support for the territorial integrity of all UN states.[^cite_note-371] The country was absent from the 2 March UN General Assembly vote condemning the invasion.[^cite_note-372] According to several reports, Morocco had authorized a secret delivery of twenty refurbished [T-72B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T-72B) tanks to Ukraine by early 2023.[^cite_note-373][^cite_note-374] In response, foreign minister [Nasser Bourita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasser_Bourita) later denied this, and said that Morocco was engaged in a policy of "[positive neutrality](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/positive%20neutrality#Noun)".[^cite_note-375] |
| [Namibia](21292.md) | Namibia called on the United Nations to peacefully resolve the situation. The Namibian Government also looked to evacuate 100 Namibians in Ukraine, should it become necessary.[^cite_note-376] |
| [Pakistan](23235.md) | Pakistan's Prime Minister, [Imran Khan](413184.md), said that conflict was not in anyone's interest. Khan also underlined Pakistan's belief that disputes should be resolved through dialogue and diplomacy.[^cite_note-377] After an appeal from 22 diplomatic missions for Pakistan to condemn Russia at the UN General Assembly was released to the public, Khan hit out at Western envoys over their inaction over [Kashmir](3030955.md), said supporting NATO in Afghanistan had backfired for Pakistan, and asked why no letter was written to appeal to India, which had also abstained.[^cite_note-378] Islamabad views Moscow as a key long-term partner in Central Asia and the Middle East, looking for increased trade and investment in the region to supplement its national development.[^cite_note-379] Pakistan's Prime Minister, [Imran Khan](413184.md) stated "We are friends of Russia, and we are also friends of the United States. We are friends of China and Europe."[^cite_note-380] Pakistan has been considered among the countries to be Russia leaning since the invasion of Ukraine according to [The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist).[^cite_note-Who_are_Russia's_supporters-72] Ambassador of Russia to Pakistan, Mr. Danila Ganich termed mutual relations between Russia and Pakistan as crucial for both countries' national interests and global stability.[^cite_note-381] |
| [Qatar](8238258.md) | Amir Sheikh [Tamim bin Hamad Al Thani](6146151.md) called on all parties to exercise restraint and resolve the dispute through constructive dialogue and diplomatic methods. He cautioned against further escalation.[^cite_note-382] A year after the invasion of Ukraine, [The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist) downgraded Qatar from "West leaning" to "neutral".[^cite_note-Who_are_Russia's_supporters-72] |
| [Saudi Arabia](349303.md) | Saudi Arabia's Deputy Foreign Minister [Waleed El Khereiji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waleed_A._Elkhereiji) and U.S. Deputy Secretary of State [Wendy Sherman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wendy_Sherman) on the first day of the invasion discussed a "strong international response to support Ukrainian sovereignty", according to the U.S. Department of State website.[^cite_note-383] Saudi Arabia remained committed to the OPEC+ agreement, Crown Prince [Mohammed bin Salman](38689358.md) told French President [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) on 27 February, in an endorsement for the bloc's alliance with Russia in its invasion of Ukraine.[^cite_note-384] |
| [South Africa](17416221.md) | [Naledi Pandor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naledi_Pandor), Minister of the [Department of International Relations and Cooperation](14036979.md) (DIRCO), blamed neither Russia nor Ukraine, calling for "inclusive talks led by the [UNSC](31956.md)" and "enhanced diplomacy".[^cite_note-385] The Head of Public Diplomacy at DIRCO said that South Africa had "adopted a [non-aligned](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement) position", discouraged the imposition of sanctions and called on NATO to respect Russian security concerns.[^cite_note-386][^cite_note-387] The [provincial cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_the_Western_Cape) of the [Western Cape Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Cape_Province) passed a resolution criticizing the Russian invasion. It called for a full withdrawal of Russian forces and criticized the ambiguous position of the South African government.[^cite_note-388] A year after the invasion of Ukraine, [The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist) downgraded South Africa from "neutral" to "Russia leaning".[^cite_note-Who_are_Russia's_supporters-72] |
| [Sri Lanka](26750.md) | The Ministry of Foreign Affairs stated that the Government of Sri Lanka was deeply concerned about the escalation of violence in Ukraine and called on all parties to exercise maximum restraint and work towards the immediate cessation of hostilities, to maintain peace, security and stability in the region, and emphasised the need for diplomacy and sincere dialogue.[^cite_note-389] |
| [Tajikistan](30108.md) | Tajikistan has decided to maintain its neutral stance in response to Russia's invasion of Ukraine, and its authorities have not yet made any official statements on the situation. Since Russia invaded Ukraine, Tajikistan has been comparatively the most exposed of the five Central Asian countries. A few other countries in the region, including Tajikistan, decided not to take part in the UNGA's emergency special session on the Russian invasion of Ukraine in March 2022. Tajikistan, in particular, is heavily reliant on Russia for its energy, economic, and security requirements. In reality, Russia is Tajikistan's most significant trading and security partner.[^cite_note-390] |
| [United Arab Emirates](69328.md) | The United Arab Emirates abstained from the vote on the U.N. Security Council resolution deploring the invasion, calling it a "foregone conclusion".[^cite_note-391] A senior Emirati diplomat [Anwar Gargash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anwar_Gargash) said that the United Arab Emirates "believes that taking sides would only lead to more violence".[^cite_note-392] The United Arab Emirates representative to the UN stating "the UAE.. articulates its firm position on the need to de-escalate and find a peaceful solution to the crisis between the parties concerned."[^cite_note-393] |
| [Uzbekistan](31853.md) | Putin with Uzbek President [Shavkat Mirziyoyev](399855.md) and other post-Soviet leaders at the [Moscow Victory Day Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Moscow_Victory_Day_Parade) on 9 May 2023Press Secretary to the President of Uzbekistan [Sherzod Asadov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sherzod_Asadov?action=edit\&redlink=1) said in a Facebook post: "The President of Russia informed the head of our state of the reasons and circumstances of the decision to launch a special operation. In his turn, the leader of Uzbekistan expressed hope that the parties will soon find mutually acceptable ways to resolve the situation and prevent its further escalation. I would like to emphasize that Uzbekistan takes a balanced, neutral position on this matter."[^cite_note-394][^cite_note-395] The Embassy of Uzbekistan in Poland is evacuating its citizens from Ukraine through Poland, Foreign Ministry of Uzbekistan said.[^cite_note-396] Upon reports of Uzbek nationals fighting in Ukraine, [Ministry of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Justice_\(Uzbekistan\)) stated that any Uzbek national found to have enlisted in the service of a foreign army or police service could face a prison term of up to five years.[^cite_note-397] On 17 March, Uzbekistan Foreign Minister [Abdulaziz Kamilov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdulaziz_Kamilov) said that he did not recognize the independence of the LPR and the DPR, supported the territorial integrity of Ukraine and wanted a peaceful solution to the situation. He added that Uzbekistan had provided humanitarian aid to Ukraine and would continue this work. He also noted that the country intended to cooperate on both sides of the conflict.[^cite_note-398] |
| [Vietnam](202354.md) | Putin and [Nguyễn Phú Trọng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nguy%E1%BB%85n_Ph%C3%BA_Tr%E1%BB%8Dng), the [General Secretary of the Communist Party of Vietnam](7482670.md) in Hanoi, Vietnam, 20 June 2024 The spokesperson for the [Vietnamese Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Vietnam\)), Lê Thị Thu Hằng, stated Vietnam "calls on parties concerned in the Ukraine crisis to exercise self-restraint, enhance dialogue efforts and step up diplomatic measures to peacefully settle differences with respect for the United Nations Charter and fundamental principles of international law, thus contributing to ensuring peace, security, and stability in the region and the world."[^cite_note-399] |
| [Zambia](34415.md) | In response to questions from an MP, Vice president [Mutale Nalumango](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mutale_Nalumango) stated that Zambia could not make a unilateral decision regarding the conflict, and that they would "not make a position as an individual country but as a grouping to various bodies that the country belongs to".[^cite_note-400] The country also made plans to evacuate all known Zambians in the country.[^cite_note-401] At the [emergency special session](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eleventh_emergency_special_session_of_the_United_Nations_General_Assembly) the Zambia's representative announced the country would support the draft resolution condemning the invasion.[^cite_note-unemsession-177] |

#### Non UN members

| State | Notes |
| --- | --- |
| [Holy See](13393.md) | [Pope Francis](1687680.md) has stated that the events in Ukraine have caused "great pain in his heart". The pope called for 2 March 2022, [Ash Wednesday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ash_Wednesday), to be a day of prayer and fasting for peace. In an unprecedented departure from diplomatic protocol, the pope went to the Russian embassy on 25 February 2022, to relay his concern over Russia's invasion of Ukraine to Moscow's ambassador.[^cite_note-402] |
| [Sovereign Military Order of Malta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sovereign_Military_Order_of_Malta) | In a press release, the Sovereign Military Order of Malta announced that they were supplying Ukraine refugees with "emergency relief, food and psychosocial support."[^cite_note-403][^cite_note-404] |

### Leans to Russia

Many of these countries are described as neutral but have remained friendly with Russia ever since it was isolated by the international community.

| State | Notes |
| --- | --- |
| [Algeria](358.md) | Putin and Algerian President [Abdelmadjid Tebboune](54133968.md) at the [St. Petersburg International Economic Forum](26267797.md), 16 June 2023The [Algerian Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Algeria\)) said it was "following developments" and called for Algerians in Ukraine to adhere to instructions given by the Algerian embassy.[^cite_note-405] The Algerian government attempted to adopt a Russia-leaning stance without explicitly expressing support for Russia's invasion. This included denying plans to replace Russian gas exports to Europe and opposing a statement that the Ukrainian embassy in Algeria had released that stated the country wanted to hire "foreign nationals to join the resistance against the Russian occupation and defend global security".[^cite_note-406][^cite_note-407] |
| [Burundi](21490998.md) | Burundi's foreign minister insisted that his country would not take sides in Russia's war against Ukraine.[^cite_note-408] However, in December 2023, Burundi reversed this position and voted against condemning Russia at the [United Nations](31769.md).[^cite_note-409] |
| [China](5405.md) | Putin with [Xi Jinping](2017814.md), the [General Secretary of the Chinese Communist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Secretary_of_the_Chinese_Communist_Party) before the [Moscow Victory Day Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Moscow_Victory_Day_Parade), 8 May 2025 [Chinese leader](262766.md) and [General Secretary of the Chinese Communist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Secretary_of_the_Chinese_Communist_Party) [Xi Jinping](2017814.md) spoke to Russian President Vladimir Putin on 25 February, calling for Russia and Ukraine to resolve the issue through negotiation.[^cite_note-410][^cite_note-411] Putin told Xi that "Russia is willing to conduct high-level negotiations with Ukraine."[^cite_note-412] China's UN Ambassador [Zhang Jun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhang_Jun_\(ambassador\)) said that "We believe that all countries should solve international disputes by peaceful means in line with the purposes and principles of the UN Charter."[^cite_note-413][^cite_note-414] In a 25 February statement, [Chinese Foreign Minister](4497735.md) and [State Councilor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Councillor) [Wang Yi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wang_Yi_\(politician\)) said that Ukraine's territory and sovereignty should be respected and urged talks as soon as possible.[^cite_note-Frank-2022-415][^cite_note-416] Ambassador to Ukraine [Fan Xianrong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fan_Xianrong) and Foreign Ministry spokesperson [Zhao Lijian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhao_Lijian) echoed the sentiment in March 2022.[^cite_note-417][^cite_note-418] Various state media in Beijing quoted Scholz's description of the day of invasion as "a terrible day for Ukraine and a dark day for Europe."[^cite_note-419][^cite_note-420][^cite_note-421] China criticized sanctions against Russia, and said it understood Russia's 'security concerns' over NATO's eastward expansion.[^cite_note-422] China's Vice Foreign Minister [Le Yucheng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Yucheng) claimed that if NATO "enlargement goes further, it would be approaching the 'outskirts of Moscow' where a missile could hit the Kremlin within seven or eight minutes."[^cite_note-423] On 22 February 2023, China blamed the invasion on the United States.[^cite_note-424] In December 2023, China voted against condemning Russia at the United Nations.[^cite_note-auto-349] |
| [Ethiopia](187749.md) | Ethiopia's Prime Minister [Abiy Ahmed](56624029.md) has called for restraint in the Ukraine conflict, noting his country's own suffering during the [Tigray War](65760352.md). The statement notably did not condemn Russia for invading its neighbor.[^cite_note-425] On 14 November 2022, Ethiopia sided with Russia on a UN General Assembly vote calling for Russia to pay war reparations to Ukraine.[^cite_note-aljazeera.com-426] Ethiopia has been considered among the countries to be Russia leaning since the invasion of Ukraine according to *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)*.[^cite_note-Who_are_Russia's_supporters-72] |
| [Laos](17752.md) | Putin and [Thongloun Sisoulith](5489678.md), the [General Secretary of the Lao People's Revolutionary Party](30663327.md) before the [Moscow Victory Day Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Moscow_Victory_Day_Parade), 8 May 2025 The Lao PDR government called on all parties to exercise utmost restraint and pursue efforts to deescalate and supported efforts to find a peaceful settlement through diplomatic means.[^cite_note-427] Laos has been considered among the countries to be Russia leaning since the invasion of Ukraine according to *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)*.[^cite_note-Who_are_Russia's_supporters-72] The Lao state-run news agency reliably repeated comments from Russian diplomats, including in a briefing in early May that "with the help of foreign countries, Ukraine has become a center of attraction for terrorists and mercenaries" and "The West is openly encouraging Kiev to attack Russia." Diplomatic, military, and trade overtures with Russia have increased.[^cite_note-428][^cite_note-429] |
| [Sudan](27421.md) | Sudan coordinated an evacuation of Sudanese citizens in Ukraine with authorities in Poland and Romania.[^cite_note-430] The Deputy Head of the Sovereign Council [Mohamed Hamdan Dagalo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohamed_Hamdan_Dagalo) stated: "Russia has the right to act in the interests of its citizens and protect its people. It has the right under the constitution and under the law. The whole world must realize that it has the right to defend its people."[^cite_note-431] Sudan has been considered among the countries to be Russia leaning since the invasion of Ukraine according to *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)*.[^cite_note-Who_are_Russia's_supporters-72] |
| [Uganda](31816.md) | [Muhoozi Kainerugaba](29207362.md), son of Ugandan President [Yoweri Museveni](293409.md) and commander of the Special Forces Command, stated that [Uganda](31816.md) could send soldiers to Russia to help Putin quell the rebellion if necessary. Uganda has been considered among the countries to be Russia leaning since the invasion of Ukraine according to *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)*.[^cite_note-Who_are_Russia's_supporters-72] |

| State | Notes |

| [Artsakh](1000530.md)[^cite_note-432] | President of Artsakh [Arayik Harutyunyan](13280780.md) welcomed the decision of President of the Russian Federation Vladimir Putin to recognize the independence of the Donetsk and Luhansk People's Republics.[^cite_note-433] The Government of the Artsakh Republic sent 14 tons of humanitarian aid to the residents of the Kyiv and Zaporizhzhia regions through Russian peacekeeping troops in Artsakh.[^cite_note-434] |
| [Transnistria](376581.md)[^cite_note-435] | On 26 February, [Vadim Krasnoselski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vadim_Krasnoselski), the president of the breakaway state of Transnistria internationally recognized as part of Moldova, in response to what he called rumors and false information that Transnistria would attack Ukraine, said that Transnistria was a peaceful state, never had plans to attack its neighbours and called those who spread these claims people without control over the situation or provocateurs with malicious intentions. Krasnoselski also noted the large ethnically [Ukrainian](145144.md) population in Transnistria and that [Ukrainian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_language) is taught in Transnistrian schools and is one of the official languages of the republic.[^cite_note-436] |

### Supports Russia

|  | This section needs to be **updated**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(July 2025)* |

| State | Notes |
| --- | --- |
| [Belarus](3457.md) | Belarus has taken an active stance of supporting Russia in its invasion of Ukraine. Belarus has let Russian troops freely use their territory to cross the border into the northern side of Ukraine (see [Belarusian involvement in the Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belarusian_involvement_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine)). |
| [Burkina Faso](3470.md) | President [Ibrahim Traoré](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibrahim_Traor%C3%A9) stated "There is a desire to change politics which leads us to turn our backs on our traditional partners and turn towards our true friends like Russia."[^cite_note-\:13-437] |
| [Central African Republic](5478.md) | On 14 November 2022, the Central African Republic sided with the Russian Federation on a UN General Assembly vote calling for Russia to pay war reparations to Ukraine.[^cite_note-aljazeera.com-426] In 2023, President [Faustin-Archange Touadéra](15370659.md) expressed support for Russian involvement in Ukraine.[^cite_note-chathamhouse.org-438] |
| [Cuba](5042481.md) | Putin and [Miguel Díaz-Canel](38630938.md), the [First Secretary of the Communist Party of Cuba](38776884.md) in Kremlin, Moscow, 9 May 2024 The Cuban government blamed the United States for the crisis in Ukraine and backed Russia's right to "self-defense", but said the conflict should be resolved diplomatically.[^cite_note-439] |
| [Eritrea](17238590.md) | Eritrea was one of five countries that voted against the [UN General Assembly resolution condemning Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_ES-11/1).[^cite_note-440] In 2023, President [Isaias Afwerki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isaias_Afwerki) expressed support for the Russian involvement in Ukraine.[^cite_note-chathamhouse.org-438][^cite_note-\:13-437] |
| [Iran](14653.md) | Putin with Iranian Supreme Leader [Ali Khamenei](385653.md) and Iranian President [Ebrahim Raisi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ebrahim_Raisi) in Tehran, Iran, 19 July 2022 Iranian president [Ebrahim Raisi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ebrahim_Raisi) phoned Putin to support stopping NATO expansion.[^cite_note-441][^cite_note-442] Iranian Foreign Minister [Hossein Amirabdollahian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hossein_Amirabdollahian) tweeted, "The #Ukraine crisis is rooted in NATO's provocations. We don't believe that resorting to war is a solution. Imperative to establish ceasefire & to find a political and democratic resolution."[^cite_note-443] Additionally, he stated that Iran would not be recognizing Crimea, or the other regions of Ukraine as property of Russia, because "Iran recognizes the sovereignty and territorial integrity of the countries."[^cite_note-444][^cite_note-445] [Mohammad Javad Larijani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Javad_Larijani), secretary of the High Council for Human Rights in Iran, told [IRNA](841314.md) that the government had not backed the military operation against Ukraine, but Tehran "has not closed eyes on the plots hatched by the US and its allies."[^cite_note-446] Supreme Leader [Ali Khamenei](385653.md) said that "in Ukraine, we are in favour of stopping the war", and that the crisis showed that the West could not be trusted, and its support for "administrations and politicians that have been installed by them" was a "mirage". He also implied that the Ukrainian government lacked the full support of its people.[^cite_note-447] He blamed US policies for the invasion and accused Washington of meddling in the "internal affairs of the country, setting up demonstrations against the government, creating velvet revolutions, creating colour coup d'etats".[^cite_note-448] |
| [Mali](19127.md) | Putin with [Assimi Goïta](65000306.md) at the [Russia–Africa Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Russia%E2%80%93Africa_Summit) in St. Petersburg, July 2023In 2023, Malian military junta leader [Assimi Goïta](65000306.md) expressed support for Russian involvement in Ukraine.[^cite_note-chathamhouse.org-438] |
| [Myanmar](19457.md) | The spokesperson for [Myanmar](19457.md)'s [State Administration Council](66589972.md), [Zaw Min Tun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zaw_Min_Tun_\(general\)), supported Russia, stating that it "was acting to protect its sovereignty" and praised Russia's role in "balancing global power".[^cite_note-449][^cite_note-450] |
| [Nicaragua](21362.md) | President [Daniel Ortega](8778.md) and his wife, Vice President [Rosario Murillo](13577522.md), expressed their support to Putin's deployment of troops to Ukraine after its recognition of the separatist Donetsk and Luhansk People's Republics. Ortega accused NATO and the United States of allegedly conspiring the 2014 [Revolution of Dignity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolution_of_Dignity) which resulted in the removal from office of president [Viktor Yanukovych](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viktor_Yanukovych).[^cite_note-451] |
| [Niger](21373.md) | Since the [2023 Nigerien coup d'état](74454838.md), the ruling [National Council for the Safeguard of the Homeland](74458151.md) has voted against condemning Russia at the [United Nations](31769.md).[^cite_note-auto-349] Under the previous government, Niger was one of 87 signatories to the UN letter condemning Russia's invasion of Ukraine.[^cite_note-Axios-71] |
| [North Korea](21255.md) | Putin with [Kim Jong Un](5962511.md), the [General Secretary of the Workers' Party of Korea](9745167.md) in Pyongyang, North Korea, 19 June 2024 [North Korea's Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(North_Korea\)) published a commentary on 26 February titled "The United States must not undermine the foundation of international peace and stability", ascribing the armed conflict to US "high-handedness and arbitrariness," and accusing Washington of pursuing "military supremacy" in disregard of Russian security concerns. In a post on the foreign ministry website, Ri Ji-song, a researcher at the Society for International Politics Study, made the remarks—the North's first public mention of the conflict following Russia's invasion of Ukraine earlier that week. "The root cause of the Ukrainian crisis also lies in the high-handedness and arbitrariness of the U.S. which has held on solely to the unilateral sanction and pressure while pursuing only global hegemony and military supremacy in disregard of the legitimate demand of Russia for its security," Ri wrote. The Yonhap News Agency quoted a North Korean studies professor at Ewha University in Seoul as calling the post a "cautious" official response from Pyongyang, since it was released in the name of an individual.[^cite_note-452] In the fall of 2024, North Korea sent military personnel to Russia who fought against Ukraine for Russia in Russian uniforms and under Russian command (see [North Korean involvement in the Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Korean_involvement_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine)). |
| [Syria](57134800.md) (before [2024](78548066.md)) | President [Bashar al-Assad](364813.md) praised the Russian invasion as a "correction of history" and accused Western nations of using "dirty methods to support terrorists in Syria and Nazis in Ukraine".[^cite_note-453] |
| [Venezuela](32374.md) (before [2026](82021123.md)) | The country's government blamed NATO and the United States for the crisis in Ukraine, stating that they had violated the [Minsk agreements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minsk_agreements). President [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md) said before the invasion was launched that Venezuela was with Putin, but also urged a diplomatic dialogue to avoid an increase in the conflict.[^cite_note-454][^cite_note-455] Maduro officially sent "a hug of solidarity" to Putin, whom he described as a "brother of Venezuela."[^cite_note-456] |
| [Zimbabwe](34399.md) | Zimbabwe evacuated 256 nationals from Ukraine, advising nationals to leave Ukraine if safe to do so.[^cite_note-457] On 14 November 2022, Zimbabwe sided with the Russian Federation on a UN General Assembly vote calling for Russia to pay war reparations to Ukraine.[^cite_note-aljazeera.com-426] In 2023, Zimbabwe's President [Emmerson Mnangagwa](6390734.md) voiced support for the Russian invasion of Ukraine.[^cite_note-chathamhouse.org-438] |

## Expulsion of diplomats

More than a dozen countries expelled Russian diplomats following the invasion, citing [espionage](10501.md) activities incompatible with diplomatic status,[^cite_note-464] and then also Russian killing of civilians in the [Bucha massacre](70455343.md).[^cite_note-aj-ger-fra-expulsions-465][^cite_note-tg-eu-further-expulsions-466] Additionally, the European Union declared 19 Russian diplomats [personae non-gratae](521241.md).[^cite_note-467]

## Intergovernmental and international organizations



&#x20; [Russia](25391.md)

---

&#x20;*Countries that have introduced sanctions on Russia in 2014:*&#x20;

&#x20; Countries that have introduced sanctions

&#x20; European Union countries that have collectively introduced sanctions

-  [United Nations](31769.md) – Secretary-General [António Guterres](471806.md) stated that the Russian invasion was "the saddest moment in my tenure" and called on Putin to withdraw his troops "in the name of humanity".

  [^cite_note-496]

  &#x20;On 25 February, the [Security Council](31956.md) failed to adopt a draft resolution which would have "deplored, in the strongest terms, the Russian Federation's aggression" on Ukraine. Of the 15 member states on the Security Council, 11 were in support, whilst three abstained from voting. The draft resolution failed due to a Russian veto.

  [^cite_note-497]

  [^cite_note-498]

  &#x20;Due to the deadlock, the Security Council passed a resolution to convene the [General Assembly](31957.md) for the [eleventh emergency special session](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eleventh_emergency_special_session_of_the_United_Nations_General_Assembly).

  [^cite_note-499]

  &#x20;On 2 March, the General Assembly voted to deplore "in the strongest possible terms" Russia's aggression against Ukraine by a vote of 141 to 5, with 35 abstentions.

  [^cite_note-generalassembly-500]

  &#x20;The [resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_ES-11/1) also called for the Russian Federation to "immediately cease its use of force against Ukraine" and "immediately, completely and unconditionally withdraw all of its military forces."

  [^cite_note-generalassembly-500]

  &#x20;Only Russia, [Belarus](3457.md), [Syria](7515849.md), [North Korea](21255.md) and [Eritrea](17238590.md) voted against the resolution.

  [^cite_note-501]

  &#x20;On 4 March, the [UN Human Rights Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Human_Rights_Council) adopted a resolution by a vote of 32 to 2, with 13 abstentions, calling for the withdrawal of Russian troops and Russian-backed armed groups from Ukraine and humanitarian access to people in need. The resolution also established a commission to investigate alleged rights violations committed during Russia's military attack on Ukraine.

  [^cite_note-502]

  &#x20;On 23 March, the UN Security Council failed to back a Russian humanitarian resolution calling for the protection of civilians and their safe passage. It was criticised by French and American representatives for not mentioning Russia's role in the ongoing crisis.

  [^cite_note-503]

  &#x20;On 24 March, the UN General Assembly voted 140 to 5 in favor of [a resolution approving aid access and civilian protection in Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_ES-11/2), which again criticized Russia's invasion of Ukraine.

  [^cite_note-504]

  &#x20;On 7 April, the UN General Assembly voted 93–24, with 58 abstentions, to [suspend Russia from the UN Human Rights Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_ES-11/3).

  [^cite_note-505]

-  [African Union](10634933.md) – [Chairman of the African Union Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chairperson_of_the_African_Union_Commission) [Moussa Faki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moussa_Faki) and Chair of the African Union and Senegalese president [Macky Sall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macky_Sall) called on Russia and "any other regional or international actor to imperatively respect international law, territorial integrity and national sovereignty of Ukraine" and urged both parties to the conflict to establish an immediate ceasefire and start political negotiations without delay.

  [^cite_note-506]

- [Arctic Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arctic_Council) – On 3 March, all member states of the Arctic Council besides Russia (Canada, Denmark, Finland, Iceland, Norway, Sweden, and the United States) released a joint statement condemning the Russian invasion of Ukraine, describing it as a flagrant violation of the organization's "core principles of sovereignty and territorial integrity". They also noted, "the grave impediments to international cooperation, including in the [Arctic](36971.md), that Russia's actions have caused". The seven members announced they would no longer attend meetings of the Council in Russia, which currently holds the organization's rotating chairmanship.

  [^cite_note-507]

-  [ASEAN](106539.md) – Foreign ministers of [ASEAN](106539.md) expressed grave concern over Russia-Ukraine tensions and urged maximum restraint and dialogue.

  [^cite_note-508]

- [Baltic Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltic_Assembly) – The Baltic Assembly published a statement in which it "firmly condemns the Russian invasion of Ukraine".

  [^cite_note-509]

  

  Putin and other leaders at the [16th BRICS summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/16th_BRICS_summit) in Kazan, Russia. Most of the [Global South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_North_and_Global_South) countries took a neutral position towards the war and maintained good relations with Russia.

  [^cite_note-510]

- [BRICS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRICS) – At the [16th BRICS Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/16th_BRICS_summit) in [Kazan](202295.md), Russia in October 2024, the Kazan Declaration emphasized adherence to the UN Charter and supported diplomatic efforts for a [peaceful resolution to the conflict in Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_negotiations_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine).

  [^cite_note-511]

-  [Caribbean Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caribbean_Community) – A statement issued on behalf of the Caribbean Community condemned the invasion of Ukraine and demanded an "immediate and complete withdrawal" of Russia's military from Ukraine.

  [^cite_note-512]

  [^cite_note-513]

-  [Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe) – The Committee of Ministers passed a resolution that "condemned in the strongest terms the armed attack on Ukraine" and called for Russia to "immediately and unconditionally cease its military operations"

  [^cite_note-514]

  &#x20;On 25 February, the [Committee of Ministers of the Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_of_Ministers_of_the_Council_of_Europe) suspended Russia from its rights of representation in the Committee of Ministers and in the [Parliamentary Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_Assembly_of_the_Council_of_Europe).

  [^cite_note-515]

  [^cite_note-516]

  &#x20;In the following days, the [European Court of Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Court_of_Human_Rights) granted interim measures indicating to Russia that it should refrain from military attacks against civilians and civilian objects and should ensure access to safe evacuation routes, healthcare, food and other essential supplies, rapid and unconstrained passage of humanitarian aid, and movement of humanitarian workers.

  [^cite_note-517]

  &#x20;Russia accused NATO and EU members of having undermined the Council of Europe, and announced its intention to withdraw from the organisation.

  [^cite_note-518]

  [^cite_note-519]

  &#x20;On 15 March, Russia notified the council of its decision to withdraw and to denounce the [European Convention on Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Convention_on_Human_Rights) by the end of 2022.

  [^cite_note-520]

  [^cite_note-521]

  &#x20;The following day, the Committee of Ministers decided to expel Russia from the Council of Europe with immediate effect.

  [^cite_note-522]

-  [European Union](9317.md) – [President of the European Commission](24468.md) [Ursula von der Leyen](2991932.md) tweeted; "We will not let President Putin tear down Europe's security architecture" and Foreign Policy Chief [Josep Borrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josep_Borrell) called on Putin to stop the "senseless aggression". President of the European Parliament, [Roberta Metsola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberta_Metsola) called for "immediate, quick, solid and swift action" and convened an extraordinary session of Parliament for 1 March.

  [^cite_note-Suliman-Pannet-2022-523]

  [^cite_note-524]

  [^cite_note-525]

- [Economic Community of West African States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_Community_of_West_African_States) – ECOWAS released a statement that condemned the invasion and called for both parties to stop the fighting. Instead, ECOWAS urged for both parties to use dialogue to solve differences for sake of the interest of peace in the region.

  [^cite_note-526]

- [International Criminal Court](14880.md) – Neither Ukraine nor Russia are parties to the [Rome Statute](68762.md), however, the Ukrainian government has voluntarily accepted the jurisdiction of the International Criminal Court since 2015 for any possible war crimes committed on Ukrainian territory since 20 February 2014.

  [^cite_note-527]

  &#x20;The [Prosecutor of the International Criminal Court](33903993.md), [Karim Ahmad Khan](11606957.md), issued a statement on 25 February reminding all parties involved that the Office of the Prosecutor (OTP) has jurisdiction to investigate any act of genocide, war crimes, or crimes against humanity and that "any person who commits such crimes, including by ordering, inciting, or contributing in another manner to the commission of these crimes, may be liable to prosecution before the Court."

  [^cite_note-528]

  &#x20;Khan issued a follow-up statement on 28 February that the OTP had a reasonable basis to begin an investigation under article 15(3) of the Rome Statute pending approval from the Pre-Trial Chamber of the court.

  [^cite_note-529]

  &#x20;The approval requirement was bypassed after 39 states parties

  [^cite_note-530]

  &#x20;referred the situation in Ukraine to the OTP and the [formal investigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Criminal_Court_investigation_in_Ukraine) commenced on 2 March.

  [^cite_note-531]

  &#x20;On 17 March 2023, International Criminal Court Issued arrest warrants for [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) and [Maria Lvova-Belova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Lvova-Belova).

  [^cite_note-iccwarrants-532]

- [International Energy Agency](285292.md) – On 25 February, the IEA's thirty-one member countries "expressed great concern over the destruction and loss of life" and said they would closely monitor impacts on energy markets.

  [^cite_note-533]

  &#x20;In response to the threat the Russian invasion posed to global oil markets, on 1 March the IEA Governing Board authorized the release of 61.7 million barrels of oil from member countries' strategic petroleum reserves. IEA Executive Director [Fatih Birol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fatih_Birol) said, "The situation in energy markets is very serious and demands our full attention. Global [energy security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_security) is under threat, putting the world economy at risk during a fragile stage of recovery."

  [^cite_note-534]

  [^cite_note-535]

  &#x20;On 1 April, the Governing Board announced a second release of emergency oil stocks, making another 120 million barrels available for oil markets.

  [^cite_note-536]

-  [NATO](21133.md) – Secretary General [Jens Stoltenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jens_Stoltenberg) condemned the attack and called it a "grave breach of international law". A separate statement announced an intention to deploy defensive forces and condemned Belarus for enabling the attack.

  [^cite_note-Suliman-Pannet-2022-523]

  &#x20;On 8 March, Stoltenberg warned that "if there is any attack against any NATO country, NATO territory, that will trigger Article 5" of the [North Atlantic Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Atlantic_Treaty).

  [^cite_note-537]

  &#x20;On 13 March, [Joe Biden](145422.md)'s national security adviser [Jake Sullivan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jake_Sullivan) warned of a full-fledged NATO response if Russia hit any part of NATO territory.

  [^cite_note-538]

-  [Nordic Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic_Council) – President [Erkki Tuomioja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erkki_Tuomioja) condemned the invasion as "completely unjust" and stated that it was "contrary to both international law and the order of European security."

  [^cite_note-539]

-  [Organization of American States](22788.md) – The [OAS](22788.md) condemned the attack as an affront to mankind and an attack on civilised international relations.

  [^cite_note-540]

  &#x20;On 21 April OAS voted to suspend Russian permanent observer status in the organization. 25 nations voted in favour, 8 abstained and none voted against the motion.

- [Organisation for Economic Co-operation and Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_for_Economic_Co-operation_and_Development) – The [OECD](33853117.md) Council formally terminated accession negotiations with Russia and ordered the closure of OECD offices in [Moscow](19004.md).

  [^cite_note-541]

-  [Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe](22796.md) – OSCE Chairman [Zbigniew Rau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zbigniew_Rau) and Secretary General [Helga Schmid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helga_Schmid) issued a joint statement on 24 February 2022 condemning the invasion.

  [^cite_note-542]

  &#x20;All international members of the multi-national [OSCE Special Monitoring Mission to Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OSCE_Special_Monitoring_Mission_to_Ukraine), which had been deployed in the country since 21 March 2014, were evacuated beginning 24 February.

  [^cite_note-543]

  &#x20;A Ukrainian member of the OSCE mission, Maryna Fenina, was killed on 1 March during the [shelling of Kharkiv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Kharkiv_\(2022\)).

  [^cite_note-544]

- [Pacific Islands Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Islands_Forum) – In a statement, Secretary-General [Henry Puna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Puna) condemned the invasion, saying the group "observed from afar the violation of Ukraine's sovereignty and territorial integrity, as well as Russia's blatant disregard for international law."

  [^cite_note-545]

### NATO

US [F-35s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-35) arrive in [Ämari Air Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%84mari_Air_Base) in Estonia on 27 February.

[^cite_note-546]

Following the 24 February 2022 [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine), eight [NATO](21133.md) member states – Bulgaria, the Czech Republic, Estonia, Latvia, Lithuania, Poland, Romania, and Slovakia – triggered security consultations under [Article 4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Atlantic_Treaty#Article_4).[^cite_note-547] The Estonian government issued a statement by Prime Minister [Kaja Kallas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaja_Kallas) saying: "Russia's widespread aggression is a threat to the entire world and to all NATO countries, and NATO consultations on strengthening the security of the Allies must be initiated to implement additional measures for ensuring the defence of NATO Allies. The most effective response to Russia's aggression is unity."[^cite_note-548] On 24 February, Stoltenberg announced new plans that "will enable us to deploy capabilities and forces, including the NATO Response Force, to where they are needed".[^cite_note-549] Following the invasion, NATO announced plans to increase military deployments[^cite_note-550] in the Baltics, Poland, and Romania.[^cite_note-551][^cite_note-552]

After the 25 February UN Security Council meeting, Stoltenberg announced that parts of the [NATO Response Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO_Response_Force) would be deployed, for the first time ever, to NATO members along the eastern border. He stated that forces would include elements of the Very High Readiness Joint Task Force (VJTF), led by France.[^cite_note-NATO_20220225-553] The US announced on 24 February that it would deploy 7,000 troops to join the 5,000 already in Europe.[^cite_note-NATO_20220225-553] NATO forces included the [USS *Harry S. Truman*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Harry_S._Truman)'s [Carrier Strike Group 8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carrier_Strike_Group_8), which entered the [Mediterranean Sea](19006.md) the previous week as part of a planned exercise. The carrier strike group was placed under NATO command, the first time this had occurred since the [Cold War](325329.md).[^cite_note-554]

As Russia began to build forces on Ukraine's border in the lead-up to the invasion, Finland and Sweden, both neutral states, increased their cooperation with NATO.[^cite_note-555] Both countries attended the emergency NATO summit as members of NATO's [Partnership for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partnership_for_Peace), and both condemned the invasion and provided assistance to Ukraine. On 25 February, Russian Foreign Ministry spokesperson [Maria Zakharova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Zakharova) threatened Finland and Sweden with "military and political consequences" if they attempted to join NATO.[^cite_note-556] Both Finnish and Swedish public opinion shifted in favour of joining NATO after the invasion.[^cite_note-557] A public petition asking the [Parliament of Finland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_Finland) to hold a referendum to join NATO reached the required 50,000 signatures, prompting a parliamentary discussion on 1 March.[^cite_note-558]



Western leaders [met in Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Brussels_extraordinary_summit) for a round of emergency summits of NATO, the European Council and the G7 to discuss the war in Ukraine, 23 March 2022

On 8 March, NATO Secretary General [Jens Stoltenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jens_Stoltenberg) warned that "any attack against any NATO country \[or] NATO territory ... will trigger Article 5 of the [North Atlantic Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Atlantic_Treaty).[^cite_note-559] On 11 March, Biden proclaimed that while the United States would, as part of NATO, "defend every single inch of NATO territory with \[its] full might", NATO would not "fight a war against Russia in Ukraine", as such "direct conflict between NATO and Russia is [World War III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_III), something we must strive to prevent".[^cite_note-560]

On 13 March, [Jake Sullivan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jake_Sullivan), the [United States National Security Advisor](44028.md) to President Biden, warned of a full-fledged NATO response if Russia were to hit any part of NATO territory.[^cite_note-561] Sullivan added on 22 March, during Biden's trip to Europe to discuss updating NATO's posture towards Russia, that Biden would emphasize three key issues: new sanctions against Russia and tightening existing sanctions, longer-term adjustments to NATO force posture and contingencies in the case of nuclear weapons use, and 'joint action' on enhancing energy security in Europe, which is highly reliant on Russian gas.[^cite_note-562] Zelenskyy repeatedly urged NATO to impose a [no-fly zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No-fly_zone) over Ukraine, which was rejected by the organization as it would involve shooting down Russian aircraft, an act that would significantly escalate the war to involve NATO.[^cite_note-563][^cite_note-564]

On 16 March, a meeting of NATO defence ministers agreed to continue supplying Ukraine with military, financial and humanitarian aid, though Stoltenberg ruled out the deployment of forces in Ukraine or a no-fly-zone, saying NATO has "a responsibility" not to escalate the war beyond Ukraine.[^cite_note-565]

Upon his arrival for the [2022 Brussels extraordinary summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Brussels_extraordinary_summit) on 24 March, Biden increased the amount of new aid offered to Ukraine by one billion dollars and announced added guarantees for NATO obligations to protect all NATO-allied nations which border Ukraine.[^cite_note-USAToday220324-566] On 28 March, Biden, at the end of his NATO trip to Europe, reaffirmed his condemnation of Putin, saying that he would "'make no apologies'" for previously stating that "'Putin cannot remain in power'".[^cite_note-567] On 29 March, Kallas sided with Biden's condemnation and called for the further isolation of Putin from international politics.[^cite_note-kallas-interview-568] As part of the [NATO Enhanced Forward Presence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO_Enhanced_Forward_Presence), NATO countries agreed to establish four multinational battalion-sized battlegroups in Bulgaria, Hungary, Romania, and Slovakia, on top of four existing battlegroups in Estonia, Latvia, Lithuania, and Poland.[^cite_note-569]

On 6 and 7 April, foreign ministers from NATO member states as well as Ukraine, the European Union, Finland, Sweden, Japan, New Zealand and Australia convened to discuss further sanctions on Russia and additional arms deliveries to Ukraine.[^cite_note-570]

### European Union

On 27 February 2022, European Commission president [Ursula von der Leyen](2991932.md) announced that the [European Union](9317.md) would ban Russian state-owned media outlets [RT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RT_\(TV_Network\)) and [Sputnik](44384287.md) in response to disinformation and their coverage of the conflict in Ukraine.[^cite_note-Politico27Feb-571] She also said that the EU would finance the purchase and delivery of military equipment to Ukraine and proposed a ban on Russian aircraft using EU airspace.[^cite_note-572] The following day, the [Council of the European Union](5866.md) adopted two assistance measures to strengthen Ukraine's military capabilities.[^cite_note-573][^cite_note-574] The measures, for a total value of €500 million, financed the provision of military equipment to the Ukrainian armed forces including – for the first time in EU history – weapons and other lethal equipment.[^cite_note-575]

On 28 February, the EU imposed a ban on transactions with the [Russian Central Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Bank_of_Russia) and a ban on the overflight of EU airspace and on access to EU airports by Russian carriers.[^cite_note-576] On 2 March, a SWIFT ban for certain Russian banks was adopted, ensuring that they were disconnected from the international financial system, and the broadcasting activities in the EU of the outlets [Sputnik](44384287.md) and [RT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RT_\(TV_network\)) were suspended.[^cite_note-577] On 10 March, additional measures targeting the Belarusian financial sector were agreed upon,[^cite_note-578] and the EU imposed restrictive measures, including an asset freeze and a travel ban on 160 prominent businesspeople ("oligarchs") and members of the [Russian Federation Council](1268578.md).[^cite_note-579] At the onset of the war, similar measures had already been applied on members of Russia's [Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Security_Council_of_Russia) and [Duma](1010187.md), and on other individuals.[^cite_note-580]

On 15 March, the EU decided to impose a fourth package of economic and individual sanctions, including trade restrictions for iron, steel, and luxury goods.[^cite_note-581] The European Commission claimed that restricting steel imports could lead to a loss of €3.3 billion in revenue for Russia,[^cite_note-582] and von der Leyen explained that the EU was working to suspend Russia's membership rights in multilateral institutions such as the [International Monetary Fund](15251.md) and the [World Bank](36752.md).[^cite_note-583] On 23 March, the Council decided to double the funding for the Ukrainian armed forces, bringing the total amount from €500 million to €1 billion.[^cite_note-584] On 1 April, [President of the European Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_the_European_Parliament) [Roberta Metsola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberta_Metsola) visited Kyiv to "show the EU's support for Ukraine" and to meet with Ukrainian officials.[^cite_note-585]

On 8 April, the EU passed the fifth round of sanctions, which included an embargo on Russian coal, restrictions on Russian-flagged ships in EU ports, restrictions on Russian and Belarusian road transport in the EU, a ban on four Russian banks, export bans of high-tech goods and expanded sanctions on family members of individuals already sanctioned.[^cite_note-586] The same day, a delegation including European Commission president Ursula von der Leyen, EU foreign policy chief [Josep Borrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josep_Borrell) and prime minister of Slovakia [Eduard Heger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eduard_Heger) visited Ukraine.[^cite_note-VDL-587] Von der Leyen presented Ukrainian president [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) with a [questionnaire to join the EU](36830143.md); she also visited the city of [Bucha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bucha,_Kyiv_Oblast) to observe the aftermath of the [Bucha massacre](70455343.md).[^cite_note-VDL-587] Borrell announced that the EU delegation to Ukraine, headed by [Matti Maasikas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matti_Maasikas), would return to Kyiv after it was evacuated at the outbreak of war.[^cite_note-588]

### International Criminal Court

On 17 March 2023, International Criminal Court (ICC) judges issued an arrest warrant for Russian leader [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) for war crimes committed during the [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine).[^cite_note-iccwarrants-532][^cite_note-589] Among the charges includes having the taking of Ukrainian children by Russian forces.[^cite_note-590] In addition to Putin, ICC also issued an arrest warrant for Russia's Presidential Commissioner for Children's Rights [Maria Lvova-Belova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Lvova-Belova) on charges of deporting Ukrainian children to Russia.[^cite_note-iccwarrants-532][^cite_note-591]

On 25 June 2024, the ICC issued an arrest warrant for Russia's former defence minister, [Sergei Shoigu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergei_Shoigu), and the chief of general staff, [Valery Gerasimov](37587470.md), on charges of alleged war crimes for [missile strikes against Ukrainian energy infrastructure](71963495.md).[^cite_note-592][^cite_note-593]

[^cite_note-81]: &#x20;["Condenamos a invasão à Ucrânia e apelamos a procura de soluções por vias diplomáticas e de diálogo. Desde 2016, Cabo Verde tem vivido momentos prolongados de crises: três anos consecutivos de seca severa; crise económica, social, energética e inflacionista associada à pandemia da COVID 19. E agora esta crise internacional perante a intervenção militar da Rússia contra a Ucrânia colocando em elevado risco a paz e a segurança globais. Tratam-se de riscos e incertezas que podem descambar em crises económicas"](https://www.facebook.com/pmucs/posts/506269537524572) \[We condemn the invasion of Ukraine and call for solutions to be sought through diplomatic channels and dialogue. Since 2016, Cape Verde has experienced prolonged periods of crisis: three consecutive years of severe drought; economic, social, energy and inflationary crisis associated with the COVID 19 pandemic. And now this international crisis in the face of Russian military intervention against Ukraine, putting global peace and security at high risk. These are risks and uncertainties that can lead to economic crises.] (in Portuguese). 24 February 2022. [Archived](https://ghostarchive.org/archive/20220408000405/https://www.facebook.com/pmucs/posts/506269537524572) from the original on 8 April 2022. Retrieved 26 February 2022 – via [Facebook](7529378.md).
